We can thus study numerically the function

f(\tau,\theta)=\frac{\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)}{\theta^{2}},

which shows that the minimal value is

f(2,\pi)=0.262163.

Figure 33
.
Plot of the function
\frac{\delta\rho(\tau;\theta)}{\theta^{2}}
for
\tau\geq 3
(Equation (
6.12
)) on the left side and of the r.h.s. of Equation (
6.13
).

Step 5: the function
\frac{\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)}{\theta^{2}}
is strictly increasing for
\tau\geq 3
.

It is enough to study the interval
\tau\in[3,4]
for the function

(6.12)

f(\tau,\theta)=\frac{\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)}{\theta^{2}}

and apply Lemma
3.14
. The previous point, plus the fact that
g
satisfies

\dot{f}(\tau)=f(\tau)-f(\tau-1),

implies that it is bounded, so we do not need to study the expansion about
\theta=0
.

The solution
\delta\rho
is written as

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)

\displaystyle=S_{0}\bigg(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1)+e^{\tau-2}\frac{(\tau-2)^{2}}{2}-e^{\tau-3}\frac{(\tau-3)^{3}}{3!}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{1}\bigg(e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}-e^{\tau-1-\tau_{1}}(\tau-1-\tau_{1})+e^{\tau-2-\tau_{1}}\frac{(\tau-2-\tau_{1})^{2}}{2}-e^{\tau-3-\tau_{1}}\frac{(\tau-3-\tau_{1})^{3}}{3!}1\!\!\mathrm{I}_{\tau\geq 3+\tau_{1}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{2}\bigg(e^{\tau-1}-e^{\tau-2}(\tau-2)+e^{\tau-3}\frac{(\tau-3)^{2}}{2}\bigg)+S_{3}\big(e^{\tau-2}-e^{\tau-3}(\tau-3)\big).

Numerical computations show that

\frac{\partial_{\tau}\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)}{\theta^{2}}\geq\frac{\partial_{\tau}\delta\rho(3,\pi)}{\pi^{2}}=0.142304,

and since the solution is continuous in the closed interval and positive at
\tau=3
from the previous point, we conclude that
\frac{\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)}{\theta^{2}}
is strictly increasing in
\tau
, see Fig.
33
.

Step 6: the asymptotic behavior.
By using Lemma
3.18
, the solution behaves as

(6.13)

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\frac{\delta\rho(\tau,\theta)e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau}}{\tau\theta^{2}}=2\frac{S_{0}(\theta)+S_{1}(\theta)+S_{2}(\theta)+S_{3}(\theta)}{\theta^{2}},

and numerical plots gives that the function is decreasing w.r.t.
\theta
values with (Fig.
33
)

0.0816077\leq 2\frac{S_{0}+S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}}{\theta^{2}}\leq 0.278936.\qed

6.1.1.
Estimate on the length for the fastest saturated spiral
\delta r(\phi;s_{0})
, segment case

In this section we study the functional

(6.14)

\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi+\bar{\alpha};s_{0})\quad\text{when}\quad\phi\geq 2\pi+\bar{\alpha},

where we denote with
\delta\tilde{L}
the variation of length: more precisely, for angles larger than
\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}
the spirals are saturated so that

\delta\tilde{L}(\phi;s_{0})=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}+\int_{\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}}^{\phi}\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi^{\prime};s_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}d\phi^{\prime}.

The first term is the due to the computation

1+\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|-\frac{r_{2}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\bar{\theta}=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}

by (
6.2
).

Using the explicit expression of
\delta\tilde{r}
of Corollary
6.5
, the length
\delta\tilde{L}
can be written by

(6.15)

\begin{split}\delta\tilde{L}(\phi;s_{0})&=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\mathcal{G}(\phi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta}+\bar{\alpha})-\mathcal{G}(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\theta_{0})\\
&\quad+\bigg(1-\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg)\mathcal{G}(\phi-2\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta})\\
&\quad+\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}H(\phi-2\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta})-\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\phi-4\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}),\end{split}

where we have used the definition of
\mathcal{G}(\phi)
. The equation it satisfies is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)-\frac{\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{H(\phi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta}+\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\frac{H(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\theta_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(1-\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg)\frac{H(\phi-2\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\mathfrak{d}(\phi-2\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\frac{H(\phi-4\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})},

which becomes for
\phi\geq\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}+4\pi+\bar{\alpha}

\frac{d}{dt}\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)-\frac{\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}+S_{L},

\displaystyle S_{L}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg[\cot(\bar{\alpha})\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-1+\bigg(1-\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg)-\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{3}}\big((1-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)\big)<0.

In particular we have for the same range of angles

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\phi}\big(\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)\big)

\displaystyle=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)\big)-\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-S_{L}

\displaystyle\geq\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)\big)-\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}.

Figure 34
.
Numerical plot of the function (
6.14
) rescaled by
e^{-\bar{c}\phi}
, divided by
\theta^{2}
and using the variable
\tau
: from left to right, the first 4 rounds, the derivative for the round
\tau\in[4,5]
and the minimal value.

In Fig.
34
the numerical plot of the function (
6.14
) shows that it is positive, increasing after
\tau=4
and thus by Lemma
3.14
and Remark
3.15
it holds

Proposition 6.8
.

It holds for the perturbation in the segment case

\delta\rho(\tau)-2.08e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau}\delta\tilde{L}(\tau)\geq 0.117\theta^{2},\quad\tau\geq 1.

6.2.
Analysis of Perturbations - Arc Case

In this section we repeat the computations in the case that the fastest saturated solution
\tilde{\xi}
for
\phi\in\phi_{0}+[0,2\pi+\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}))
has the following structure:

•

it is an arc of the level set
\{u=u(\bar{\zeta}(s_{0}))\}
, starting in
P_{0}=\bar{\zeta}(\phi_{0})
and ending in
P_{1}=\tilde{\zeta}(\phi_{1})=\tilde{\zeta}(\phi_{0}+\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\Delta\phi}})
;

•

a segment
[P_{1},P_{2}]
tangent to the level set
\{u=u(\bar{\zeta}(s_{0}))\}
in
P_{1}
and such that
P_{2}=\tilde{\zeta}(\phi_{2})=\bar{\zeta}(\phi_{1}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha})
is saturated;

•

a saturated spiral with
\beta(\phi)=\bar{\alpha}
for
\phi\geq\phi_{2}
.

Due to the presence of the initial arc, we call it the
arc case
. The analysis of this section refers to the geometric situation of Figure
35
.

We observe that for angles
\phi\geq\phi_{1}
the spiral has the same structure of the segment case (Subsection
6.1
), with
\bar{\theta}=\frac{\pi}{2}
, though we will show that the analysis of the derivative is different due to the presence of the initial arc: the formulas will coincide when
\bar{\theta}=\frac{\pi}{2}
and
\Delta\phi=0
, yielding as corollary that the derivative
\delta r
is continuous across
\bar{\theta}=\frac{\pi}{2}
(Remark
6.12
).

Figure 35
.
Analysis of perturbations of the arc case, Subsection
6.2
.

Let us denote as before

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{thetapertsegm}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\theta}}=\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta_{0}

We compute now the variations up to second order terms w.r.t.
\delta s_{0}
:

\delta P_{0}=\delta s_{0}e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta)},\quad\delta\phi_{0}=\delta s_{0}\frac{\cos(\theta)}{r(\phi_{0})},

(6.16)

\delta P_{1}=\tilde{r}(\phi_{1})\delta\phi_{1}e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2})}+\delta s_{0}\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi)},

(6.17)

\delta P_{2}=\delta P_{1}+\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2})}-|P_{2}-P_{1}|e^{i\phi_{1}}\delta\phi_{1}.

By the saturation condition on
P_{2}
and
P_{2}^{\prime}
we find that

0=u(P_{2}^{\prime})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})(\delta s_{0}+L(P^{\prime}_{0}P^{\prime}_{1})+|P_{2}^{\prime}-P_{1}^{\prime}|+L(P_{0})),

where
L(P_{0})
is the length of the spiral up to the angle
\phi_{0}
and
L(P^{\prime}_{0}P^{\prime}_{1})
is the length of the arc
P^{\prime}_{0}P_{1}^{\prime}
, in particular

\displaystyle 0=e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha})}\cdot\delta P_{2}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})(\delta s_{0}+\delta L(P_{0}P_{1})+\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|).

We now notice that

\delta L(P_{0}P_{1})=\delta s_{0}\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi+\tilde{r}(\phi_{1})\delta\phi_{1}-\delta s_{0}\cos(\theta),

so that the saturation condition becomes

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\tilde{r}(\phi_{1})\cos(\bar{\alpha})\delta\phi_{1}+\delta s_{0}\sin(\theta)\sin(\bar{\alpha})+\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|\cos(\bar{\alpha})-|P_{2}-P_{1}|\delta\phi_{1}\sin(\bar{\alpha})

\displaystyle\quad-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\big(\delta s_{0}+\delta s_{0}\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi+\tilde{r}(\phi_{1})\delta\phi_{1}-\delta s_{0}\cos(\theta)+\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|\big)

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\big(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi\big)-|P_{2}-P_{1}|\delta\phi_{1}\sin(\bar{\alpha}),

in particular

(6.18)

|P_{2}-P_{1}|\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta s_{0}}=\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big).

Moreover, from the relations

r(\phi_{2})\delta\phi_{2}=\delta P_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\pi-\bar{\alpha})}\quad\text{and}\quad\phi_{2}=\phi_{1}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha},

we obtain
\delta\phi_{2}=\delta\phi_{1}
and

(6.19)

\begin{split}\frac{r(\phi_{2})\delta\phi_{1}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|-r(\phi_{1})\delta\phi_{1}&=\cot(\bar{\alpha})|P_{2}-P_{1}|\delta\phi_{1}-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)\delta s_{0}\\
&=-\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big).\end{split}

The variation of the angle
\Delta\phi
along the arc of the level set is then

(6.20)

\begin{split}\delta\Delta\phi&=\delta\phi_{1}-\delta\phi_{0}\\
&=\frac{\delta s_{0}}{|P_{2}-P_{1}|}\left(\sin(\theta)(1-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta))\right)-\frac{\delta s_{0}}{r(\phi_{0})}\cos(\theta).\end{split}

The variation
\delta P(\phi)
along the segment
[P_{1},P_{2}]
can be computed as

\displaystyle\delta P(\phi)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta P_{1}\cdot e^{\imath\phi_{1}}-|P(\phi)-P_{1}|\delta\phi_{1}}{\cos(\phi-\phi_{1})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\cos(\phi-\phi_{1})}\bigg(\sin(\theta)\bigg(1-\frac{|P(\phi)-P_{1}|}{|P_{2}-P_{1}|}\bigg)+\frac{|P(\phi)-P_{1}|}{|P_{2}-P_{1}|}\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\bigg),

with
\phi\in(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
.

Remark 6.9
.

We remark that
\Delta\phi\leq\tan\bar{\alpha},
where the equality corresponds to the situation of the case study of Section
5
with
\mathtt{a}=0
. Indeed for such a choice

|P_{2}-P_{1}|\frac{\delta\Delta\phi}{\delta s_{0}}=-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta))-\frac{|P_{2}-P_{1}|}{\tilde{r}(\phi_{0})}\cos(\theta)<0

unless
\theta=0
and
|P_{2}-P_{1}|=0
. Notice that for or
\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}}
we do not have the initial segment
[(0,0),(1,0)]
, and this is the reason why the limit value
\Delta\phi=\tan(\bar{\alpha})
is allowed.

To compute
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
for
\phi\geq\phi_{2}
, we observe that the angle
\beta(\phi)
is equal to
\bar{\alpha}
in this region, so that one obtains

Lemma 6.10
.

The RDE satisfied by
\tilde{r}
for
\phi\geq\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}
is

(6.21)

\dot{\tilde{r}}(\phi;s_{0})=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})-\begin{cases}R(\phi)&\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}\leq\phi<\phi_{0}+2\pi+\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}),\\
0&\phi_{0}+2\pi+\beta^{-}(\phi_{0})\leq\phi<\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2},\\
\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2})&\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}\leq\phi\leq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi,\\
|P_{2}-P_{1}|\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi}+\frac{\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}&\phi\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi.\end{cases}

where
R(\phi)
is the radius of curvature of
r(\phi)
(the original spiral).

Proof.

We have just to compute the radius of curvature, since
\beta(\phi)=\bar{\alpha}
fixed for
\phi\geq\phi_{2}
. Formula (
6.21
) follows easily, observing that if
\beta^{-}(s_{0})<\frac{\pi}{2}
then there is corner in the spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
whose subdifferential is

\partial^{-}\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})=\bigg\{e^{i\phi},\phi\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bigg[\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}),\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg]\bigg\}.

For the angles
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}\leq\phi\leq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi
the radius of curvature is precisely the radius of the spiral, computed at the previous angle (Formula (
3.1
)). The jump at
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi
is due to the segment
[P_{1},P_{2}]
, corresponding in a jump in
s^{-}(\phi)
at that angle.
∎

Computing the derivative w.r.t.
s_{0}
for
\phi>\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}
we can prove the following

Proposition 6.11
.

For
\phi>\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}
, the derivative

\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})=\lim_{\delta s_{0}\searrow 0}\frac{\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0}+\delta s_{0})-\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})}{\delta s_{0}}

satisfies the RDE on
\mathbb{R}

\frac{d}{d\phi}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})-\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}+S(\phi;s_{0}),

with source

\displaystyle S(\phi;s_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}}-\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta}+\cos(\theta)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\sin(\theta)1\!\!\mathrm{I}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+[0,\Delta\phi]}-\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))\big)\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi}.

We recall the notation

\phi_{1}=\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi,\quad\phi_{2}=\phi_{1}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}.

which we will use in the following.

Proof.

We compute first the variation of the initial data of (
6.21
): observing that the spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
is saturated at
\phi_{2}
and hence its tangent vector is
e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2})}
, one has up to second order terms for
\phi
close to
\phi_{2}

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{r}(\phi_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta P_{2}\cdot e^{i\phi_{1}}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta s_{0}\sin(\theta)-|P_{2}-P_{1}|\delta\phi_{1}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)),

which gives the first jump at
\phi_{2}
.

Being
R(\phi)
the same up to
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}
, the RDE for
\delta\tilde{r}
is actually the ODE

\frac{d}{d\phi}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)

for
\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}\leq\phi<\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}
.

Using
R=Ds^{-}
and taking
\delta\phi\ll 1
, the variation at
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}
is up to second order terms w.r.t.
\delta\phi

\displaystyle\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}+\delta\phi;s_{0}+\delta s_{0})-\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}+\delta\phi;s_{0})

\displaystyle=(1+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\phi)\big(\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0};s_{0}+\delta s_{0})-\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0};s_{0})\big)-\big(s^{-}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}+\delta\phi)-s^{-}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0})\big)

\displaystyle=(1+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\phi)\big(\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0};s_{0}+\delta s_{0})-\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0};s_{0})\big)-\delta s_{0}.

Dividing by
\delta s_{0}
, letting
\delta s_{0}\searrow 0
first and then
\phi\searrow 0
, we conclude that
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
has a jump of size
-1
at the angle
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}
.

From (
6.21
) it follows that

\frac{d}{d\phi}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)

for
\phi\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\theta_{0},\frac{\pi}{2}]
.

At about the angle
\phi=\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}
, we can repeat the analysis for a small angle as we did at the angle
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}
above, obtaining that
there is an upward jump of value
\cos(\theta)
, giving the source
\cos(\theta)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}}
.

Again, from (
6.21
) it follows that

\frac{d}{d\phi}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)-\sin(\theta),

for
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}<\phi<\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi
, because
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}+[0,\Delta\phi))=\sin(\theta)
.

At the angle
\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi
the solution has a discontinuity due to the variation of
\delta\phi_{1}
and
|P_{2}-P_{1}|
: computing the first order approximations for
0\leq\delta\phi\ll 1
one gets

\displaystyle\tilde{r}\bigg(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi+\delta\phi,s_{0}+\delta s_{0}\bigg)-\tilde{r}\bigg(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi+\delta\phi,s_{0}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\left[\tilde{r}\bigg(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi-\delta\phi,s_{0}+\delta s_{0}\bigg)-\tilde{r}\bigg(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi-\delta\phi,s_{0}\bigg)\right](1+2\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\phi)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-|P^{\prime}_{2}-P^{\prime}_{1}|H(\phi-\phi_{2}+\delta\phi_{1})-(\delta\phi+\delta\phi_{1})\tilde{r}(\phi_{1},s_{0}+\delta s_{0})-(\delta\phi-\delta\phi_{1})\frac{\tilde{r}(\phi_{2},s_{0}+\delta s_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+|P_{2}-P_{1}|H(\phi-\phi_{2})+\delta\phi\tilde{r}(\phi_{1},s_{0})+\delta\phi\frac{\tilde{r}(\phi_{2},s_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})},

with
H(\phi)
being the Heaviside function. Dividing by
\delta s_{0}
, using that
\delta\tilde{(}\phi_{1})=\sin(\theta)
and passing to the limit
\delta s_{0}\searrow 0
we obtain

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{r}\bigg(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi+\delta\phi;s_{0}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\delta\tilde{r}\bigg(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi-\delta\phi;s_{0}\bigg)\big(1+2\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\phi\big)-\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi_{2};s_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\phi-\sin(\theta)\delta\phi

\displaystyle\quad\quad+|P_{2}-P_{1}|\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta s_{0}}\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi}+\bigg[-\frac{\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|}{\delta s_{0}}-\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta s_{0}}\tilde{r}(\phi_{1})+\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta{s_{0}}}\frac{\tilde{r}(\phi_{2})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg].

Hence its derivative w.r.t.
\phi
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\phi}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})

\displaystyle=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})-\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha};s_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg(-\frac{\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|}{\delta s_{0}}+\frac{\tilde{r}(\phi_{2};s_{0})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta s_{0}}-\tilde{r}(\phi_{1})\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta s_{0}}\bigg)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi}+|P_{2}-P_{1}|\frac{\delta\phi_{1}}{\delta s_{0}}\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi},

where
\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}
is the derivative of the Dirac’s delta:

\langle\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{x},f\rangle=-\frac{df(x)}{dx}.

Using (
6.18
) and (
6.19
) we obtain source in the statement.

For
\phi\geq\phi_{2}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}
the equation is linear, and then the conclusion follows.
∎

We remark that, since

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\left(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\right)\geq 0,

being
\Delta\phi\geq 0
, it follows that the perturbation is positive for
\phi\in[0,\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi-\bar{\alpha}]
, as the optimality of Theorem
4.12
implies.

Remark 6.12
.

Observe that when
\Delta\phi=0
the source becomes

\displaystyle S(\phi;s_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta))\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}}-\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\theta_{0}}

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\cos(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}(1-\cos(\theta))\big)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}}+\big(\sin(\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta))\big)\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}},

which is the same as the source of the segment case. Hence the derivative
\delta r
is continuous when passing from the segment case to the arc case, i.e. when
\bar{\theta}=\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Using the kernel
G(\phi)
of Remark
3.21
we obtain the following explicit representation of the solution
\delta\tilde{r}
.

Corollary 6.13
.

The solution
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)
for
\phi\geq\phi_{2}=\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi
is explicitly given by

\begin{split}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\left(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\right)G\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-\Delta\phi-\frac{\pi}{2}+\bar{\alpha}\bigg)-G(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\theta_{0})\\
&\quad+\cos(\theta)G\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg)-\sin(\theta)M\bigg(\phi;\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2};\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi\bigg)\\
&\quad+\bigg(-\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))\big)\bigg)G\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\Delta\phi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg)\\
&\quad+\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))\big)\mathfrak{d}_{\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi}\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))\big)G\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\Delta\phi-\frac{\pi}{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}\bigg).\end{split}

We next make the change of variables

\delta\tilde{r}(\phi_{2}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau;s_{0})=\delta\rho(\tau)e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau},\quad\phi_{2}=\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha},

obtaining the following corollary.

Corollary 6.14
.

The values of the perturbation depend only on the angles
\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta_{0}
and
\Delta\phi
, and the function
\delta\rho(\tau)
is given by

\delta\rho(\tau)=S_{0}g(\tau)+S_{1}g(\tau-\tau_{1})+S_{2}g(\tau-\tau_{2})+S_{3}m(\tau,\tau_{2},1)+S_{4}g(\tau-1)+D\mathfrak{d}_{1}+S_{5}g(\tau-2),

where

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{tau1}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tau_{1}}}=1-\frac{\Delta\phi+\theta}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}},\quad\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{tau2}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tau_{2}}}=1-\frac{\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}},

and

S_{0}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big),

S_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=-e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau_{1}}=-\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}e^{\bar{c}(\Delta\phi+\theta)},

S_{2}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=\cos(\theta)e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau_{2}}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})\cos(\theta)}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}e^{\bar{c}\Delta\phi},

S_{3}(\theta)=-\sin(\theta),\qquad m(\tau,\tau_{2},1)=\int_{\tau_{2}}^{1}g(\tau-\tau^{\prime})e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau^{\prime}}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})d\tau^{\prime},

S_{4}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\Big(-2\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))+\sin(\theta)\Big)

D(\theta,\Delta\phi)=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))\big),

S_{5}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=-\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\Big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta))\Big).

The next result is essential for the analysis in the following sections: as in the previous case we let
\theta
to vary in
[0,\pi]
, even if for spiral strategies
\theta\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Proposition 6.15
.

The function
\delta\rho=\delta\rho(\tau;\theta,\Delta\phi)
is strictly positive outside the region

N

arc

\displaystyle=\{\theta=0\}\cup\big\{\tau_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)\leq\tau<\tau_{2}(\Delta\phi),0\leq\theta\leq\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h1Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{1}(\Delta\phi)}}\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\big\{\tau_{2}(\Delta\phi)\leq\tau<1,0\leq\theta\leq\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h2Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{2}(\Delta\phi)}}\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\cup\big\{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{tau3thetaDeltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tau_{3}(\theta,\Delta\phi)}}\leq\tau<1,h_{2}(\Delta\phi)\leq\theta<\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h3Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{3}(\Delta\phi)}}\big\}\cup\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Nresarc}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N_{\mathrm{res}}}},

where the functions
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h1Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{1}(\Delta\phi)}},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h2Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{2}(\Delta\phi)}},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h3Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{3}(\Delta\phi)}},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{tau3thetaDeltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\tau_{3}(\theta,\Delta\phi)}}
are determined by solving the equations

•

for
\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}
, the function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
is determined implicitly by

(6.22)

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)}-1=0,

•

for
\tau_{2}\leq\tau\leq 1
, the function
h_{2}(\Delta\phi)
is determined by

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi)}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\cos(\theta)=0,

•

for
\tau_{3}\leq\tau\leq 1
, the function
h_{3}(\Delta\phi)
is determined by

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi)}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\cos(\theta)-\sin(\theta)\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}=0,

•

the function
\tau_{3}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=1-\frac{\theta_{3}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}
is given by solving in the region
\{h_{2}(\Delta\phi)\leq\theta\leq h_{3}(\Delta\phi)\}

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\theta_{3})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta-\theta_{3})}+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi-\theta_{3}}-\sin(\theta)M(\Delta\phi-\theta_{3};0,\Delta\phi)=0.

Finally, the region
N_{\mathrm{res}}
is contained in the set

N_{\mathrm{res}}\subset\big\{\tau\geq 3,\theta\in(3.12,\pi],\Delta\phi\in(2.37,\tan(\bar{\alpha})]\big\}\cup\bigg\{\tau=1,\Delta\phi\geq\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\theta)}\bigg\}.

The asymptotic behavior is given by

(6.23)

\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\frac{\delta\rho(\tau)e^{-\bar{c}\tau}}{\tau\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}=\frac{2}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\bigg(S_{0}+S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau_{2}}-e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}}{\bar{c}}+S_{4}+S_{5}\bigg).

The region of negativity for the arc case is depicted in Fig.
36
in the first 3 rounds: the last region is due to the fact that the sum of the sources in (
6.23
) is negative in some region, see Fig.
47
below. We will not use this region in our perturbations.

Figure 36
.
The region where
\delta\tilde{r}\leq 0
in the arc case for
\tau\in[0,1]
, neglecting
N_{\mathrm{res}}
for clarity: in addition to the plane
\{\theta=0\}
, it contains the cyan region
\{0\leq\theta\leq h_{1}(\Delta\phi),\tau_{1}\leq\tau\leq\tau_{2}\}
and the trapezoidal blue region for
\{\tau_{2}\leq\tau\leq 1\}
.

Proof.

The proof will be given in several steps.

Case 0:
\theta=0
.

It is easy to see that in this case
\delta\rho(\tau)=0
, so that
\{\theta=0\}\subset N_{\mathrm{arc}}
.

Case 1:
\tau\in[0,\tau_{1})
.

Being
S_{0}>0
for
\theta>0
, it follows that
\delta\rho(\tau)>0
in this region.

Case 2:
\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
.

The solution
\delta r(\phi;s_{0})
is written explicitly as

\displaystyle\delta r(\phi;s_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi+\theta}

\displaystyle=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi+\theta}\bigg(\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)}-1\bigg).

The study of the above equation is done in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.16
.

The equation

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)}-1=0

determines implicitly a convex function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
such that

h_{1}(\Delta\phi)<\hat{\theta}\quad\text{unless}\ \Delta\phi=0,

and moreover

\Delta\phi\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\Delta\phi):=\Delta\phi+h_{1}(\Delta\phi)

is strictly increasing unless
\Delta\phi=0
and has derivative
<1
.

(a)

Plot of the function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
of Lemma
6.16
.

(b)

Plot of the function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)+\Delta\phi
of Lemma
6.16
.

Figure 37
.
Numerical plots of the function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
of Lemma
6.16
.

A numerical plot of
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
is in Fig.
37(a)
, while in Fig.
37(b)
it is plotted the function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)+\Delta\phi
.

Proof.

Let

(6.24)

f_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)}-1.

Taking the derivative w.r.t.
\theta
we obtain

(6.25)

\begin{split}\partial_{\theta}f_{1}&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\big(\sin(\theta)+\cos(\theta)\Delta\phi-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)\big)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)}\\
&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\big(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\Delta\phi\big)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)}.\end{split}

Since numerically one observe that (see Fig.
38(b)
)

f_{1}\bigg(\bigg[\frac{\pi}{4},\pi\bigg]\times[0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})]\bigg)\geq f_{1}(\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))=0.971097,

\partial_{\theta}f_{1}\bigg(\bigg[0,\frac{\pi}{4}\bigg],\Delta\phi\bigg)\geq\partial_{\theta}f_{1}\bigg(\frac{\pi}{4},0\bigg)\geq 1.29579(\theta+\Delta\phi),

then
\partial_{\theta}f_{1}>0
in the region
(0,\frac{\pi}{4})\times[0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})]
(observe that
f1(0,\Delta\phi)=-1
). We deduce that the equation
f_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)=0
defines an implicit function
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
, such that

h_{1}(\Delta\phi)<\hat{\theta}\quad\text{unless}\ \Delta\phi=0.

(a)

Plot of the function
f_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)
, Equation (
6.24
), in the region
[\frac{\pi}{4},\pi]\times[0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})]
.

(b)

Plot of the function
\partial_{\theta}f_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)
, Equation (
6.25
), in the region
[0,\frac{\pi}{4}]\times[0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})]
.

(c)

Plot of the function (
6.26
).

(d)

Plot of the function (
6.27
).

Figure 38
.
Numerical plots for the proof of Lemma
6.16
.

By the Implicit Function Theorem we obtain

(6.26)

\begin{split}\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\phi}&=-\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\Delta\phi}\\
&=-1+\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})\cos(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\Delta\phi}.\end{split}

so that in the region of interest one can check numerically that (see Fig.
38(c)
)

-1\leq\frac{dh_{1}(\Delta\phi)}{d\Delta\phi}<-1+\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})\cos(\hat{\theta})\tan(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})\tan(\bar{\alpha})}=0.0251844.

Differentiating w.r.t
\Delta\phi
one obtains

(6.27)

\begin{split}\frac{d^{2}h_{1}}{d\Delta\phi^{2}}&=\partial_{\Delta\phi}\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\phi}+\partial_{\theta}\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\phi}\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\phi}\\
&=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})\cos(\theta)(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))}{(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\Delta\phi)^{2}}\\
&\quad+\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi(-\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)+\cos(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi)}{(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\Delta\phi)^{2}}\bigg(-1+\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})\cos(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\Delta\phi}\bigg),\end{split}

and numerically one sees that (see Fig.
38(d)
)

\frac{d^{2}h_{1}}{d\Delta\phi^{2}}>0.0168525,

so that the function is convex, with a minimum near
\Delta\phi=2.35681
.

Finally, the only case for which
\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\phi}=-1
is for
\Delta\phi=0
,
g_{3}(\Delta\phi)=\hat{\theta}
. This means that the function

\delta\phi\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\Delta\phi)=\Delta\phi+h_{1}(\Delta\phi)

is strictly increasing, convex and has derivative
0
for
\Delta\phi=0
, see Fig.
37(b)
.
∎

Case 3:
\tau\in[\tau_{2},1)
.

The solution to be studied is

\displaystyle\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi+\theta)}

\displaystyle\quad+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi)}-\sin(\theta)\int_{\phi_{2}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi}^{\phi}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi^{\prime})}d\phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\theta)}\bigg[\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\theta)}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\cos(\theta)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad-\sin(\theta)\tan(\bar{\alpha})\big(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\theta)}-1\big).

At the initial angle
\phi=\phi_{2}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi
we have

(6.28)

\begin{split}f_{2}(\theta,\Delta\phi)&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi)}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\cos(\theta)\\
&=f_{1}(\theta,\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\cos(\theta).\end{split}

Numerical plots (see Fig.
39(a)
) show that
f_{2}<0
is a triangular shaped region
N_{2}
with corners

(0,0),\quad(0.0956622,0),\quad(0,0.0428438).

(a)

Plot of the function
f_{2}(\theta,\Delta\phi)
(
6.28
).

(b)

Plot of the function
f_{3}(\theta,\Delta\phi)
(
6.29
).

(c)

Plot of the function (
6.30
).

(d)

Plot of
f_{2}(\cdot/3)=0
(blue),
f_{3}=0
(orange),
f_{4}(\cdot/3)=0
(green). We have rescaled the first and the third for clarity.

Figure 39
.
Numerical plots for the analysis of the negative region with
\tau\in[\tau_{2},1]
.

Next, we take the derivative of
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
w.r.t.
\phi
, obtaining

(6.29)

\begin{split}\partial_{\phi}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})&=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})\bigg[\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi)}\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\cos(\theta)-\sin(\theta)\tan(\bar{\alpha})\bigg]\\
&=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\phi-\phi_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\Delta\phi)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})f_{3}(\theta,\Delta\phi).\end{split}

Numerically (see Fig.
39(b)
), the negativity region is a triangular shaped region
N_{3}
with corners

(0,0),\quad(0.639078,0),\quad(0,0.329784).

Being this region larger than the negativity region for
f_{2}
, it follows that

\big\{\tau\in[\tau_{2},1],(\theta,\Delta\phi)\in N_{2}\big\}\subset N_{\mathrm{arc}},

and

\big\{\tau\in[\tau_{2},1],(\theta,\Delta\phi)\notin N_{3}\big\}\cap N_{\mathrm{arc}}=\emptyset.

In particular the derivative of
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
is strictly negative in the region of interest, and thus this determines a function
\tau_{2}(\theta,\Delta\phi)
whose graph is the boundary of the region of negativity.

The maximal region is computed by considering

(6.30)

\begin{split}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi_{2}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-;s_{0})&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\\
&\quad+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-\sin(\theta)\tan(\bar{\alpha})\big(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1\big)\\
&=f_{4}(\theta,\Delta\phi).\end{split}

Numerically (see Fig.
39(c)
) the negativity region is triangular shaped with corners

(0,0),\quad(0,0.0478458),\quad(0.0956622,0).

The contours of the three regions of negativity are in Fig.
39(d)
.

Case 4:
\tau\in[1,1+\tau_{1})

The solution
\delta\rho
is

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau)

\displaystyle=S_{0}(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1))+S_{1}e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}+S_{2}e^{\tau-\tau_{2}}+S_{3}e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\tau-1}+S_{4}e^{\tau-1}

\displaystyle=e^{\tau-1}\bigg[S_{0}(e-(\tau-1))+S_{1}e^{1-\tau_{1}}+S_{2}e^{1-\tau_{2}}+S_{3}e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}+S_{4}\bigg].

The worst case is for
\tau=1+\tau_{1}
, so that we have to study the function

\displaystyle f(\theta,\Delta\phi)

\displaystyle=S_{0}(e-\tau_{1})+S_{1}e^{1-\tau_{1}}+S_{2}e^{1-\tau_{2}}+S_{3}\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}+S_{4}.

One observes numerically that the quantity inside the bracket is strictly positive (
>0.44
), and also numerically one obtains that

\frac{f(\theta,\Delta\phi)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq\frac{f(\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}{\pi(\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha})}=0.031,

and that (see Fig.
40
)

\frac{\delta\rho([1,1+\tau_{1}),\theta,\Delta\phi)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq\frac{\delta\rho(1+\tau_{1},\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}{\pi(\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha})}>0.04.

.

for

Case 5:
\tau\in 1+[\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
.

The solution is given by

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau)

\displaystyle=S_{0}(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1))+S_{1}\big(e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-1}(\tau-\tau_{1}-1)\big)+S_{2}e^{\tau-\tau_{2}}+S_{3}e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\tau-1}+S_{4}e^{\tau-1}

\displaystyle=e^{\tau-1}\bigg[S_{0}(e-(\tau-1))+S_{1}(e^{1-\tau_{1}}-e^{-\tau_{1}}(\tau-\tau_{1}-1))+S_{2}e^{1-\tau_{2}}+S_{3}e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}+S_{4}\bigg].

A numerical evaluation shows that (see Fig.

)

\frac{\delta\rho([1+\tau_{1},1+\tau_{2}),\theta,\Delta\phi)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq\frac{\delta\rho(\tau_{1},\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}{\pi(\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}>0.04.

Case 6:
\tau\in[1+\tau_{2},2]
.

The solution is given by

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau)

\displaystyle=S_{0}(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1))+S_{1}\big(e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-1}(\tau-\tau_{1}-1)\big)+S_{2}\big(e^{\tau-\tau_{2}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{2}-1}(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{3}e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\bigg(\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\tau-1}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2+\frac{\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}+e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}}{(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}e^{\tau-2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{4}e^{\tau-1}.

Numerically one observes that (see Fig.

)

\frac{\delta\rho([1+\tau_{2},2),\theta,\Delta\phi)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq\frac{\delta\rho(1+\tau_{2},\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}{\pi(\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}>0.04.

Case 7:
\tau\in[2,2+\tau_{1}]
.

The explicit form of the solution is

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau)

\displaystyle=S_{0}\bigg(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1)+e^{\tau-2}\frac{(\tau-2)^{2}}{2}\bigg)+S_{1}\big(e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-1}(\tau-\tau_{1}-1)\big)+S_{2}\big(e^{\tau-\tau_{2}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{2}-1}(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{3}\bigg[\frac{e^{-\bar{c}\tau}}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}\big(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-1)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad+\frac{e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-1)}}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\bigg(-e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)+e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+S_{4}\big(e^{\tau-1}-e^{\tau-2}(\tau-2)\big)+S_{5}e^{\tau-2}.

Numerical evaluations show that (see Fig.
43
)

\frac{\delta\rho([2,2+\tau_{1}),\theta,\Delta\phi)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq\frac{\delta\rho(2,\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}{\pi(\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}>0.04,

so that the function is positive.

The explicit form of the solution is

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau)

\displaystyle=S_{0}\bigg(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1)+e^{\tau-2}\frac{(\tau-2)^{2}}{2}\bigg)+S_{1}\bigg(e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-1}(\tau-\tau_{1}-1)+e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-2}\frac{(\tau-2-\tau_{1})^{2}}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{2}\big(e^{\tau-\tau_{2}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{2}-1}(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)\big)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{3}\bigg[\frac{e^{-\bar{c}\tau}}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}\big(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-1)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad+\frac{e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-1)}}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\bigg(-e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)+e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+S_{4}\big(e^{\tau-1}-e^{\tau-2}(\tau-2)\big)+S_{5}e^{\tau-2}.

Numerical evaluations show that (see Fig.
44
)

\frac{\delta\rho([2+\tau_{1},2+\tau_{2}),\theta,\Delta\phi)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq\frac{\delta\rho(2+\tau_{1},\pi,\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}{\pi(\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}>0.04,

so that the function is positive.

\displaystyle\delta\rho(\tau)

\displaystyle=S_{0}\bigg(e^{\tau}-e^{\tau-1}(\tau-1)+e^{\tau-2}\frac{(\tau-2)^{2}}{2}\bigg)+S_{1}\bigg(e^{\tau-\tau_{1}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-1}(\tau-\tau_{1}-1)+e^{\tau-\tau_{1}-2}\frac{(\tau-2-\tau_{1})^{2}}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{2}\bigg(e^{\tau-\tau_{2}}-e^{\tau-\tau_{2}-1}(\tau-\tau_{2}-1)+e^{\tau-\tau_{2}-2}\frac{(\tau-\tau_{2}-2)^{2}}{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+S_{3}\bigg[\frac{e^{-\bar{c}\tau}}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}\big(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-1)}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)+e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-1}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\frac{e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-2)}}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{3}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\bigg(e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-2)}\frac{(\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-2))^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-2)}\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-2)+e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau-\tau_{2}-2)}-1\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+S_{4}\big(e^{\tau-1}-e^{\tau-2}(\tau-2)\big)+S_{5}e^{\tau-2}.

so that the function is positive.

and

we obtain
