6.2.1.
Estimate on the length for the fastest saturated spiral
\delta r(\phi;s_{0})
, arc case

We will repeat some of the estimates for the functional

\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi;s_{0}),\quad\phi\geq\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}+2\pi.

Using computations similar to Section
6.1.1
we obtain

(6.32)

\begin{split}\delta\tilde{L}(\phi;s_{0})&=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\\
&\quad+\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\left(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\right)\mathcal{G}\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-\Delta\phi-\frac{\pi}{2}+\bar{\alpha}\bigg)-\mathcal{G}(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\theta_{0})\\
&\quad+\cos(\theta)\mathcal{G}\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg)-\sin(\theta)\bigg(\mathfrak{G}\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg)-\mathfrak{G}\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}-\Delta\phi\bigg)\bigg)\\
&\quad+\bigg(-\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\Big)\bigg)\mathcal{G}\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\Delta\phi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg)\\
&\quad+\Big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\Big)H\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}+2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi\bigg)\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\Big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\Big)\mathcal{G}\bigg(\phi-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\Delta\phi-\frac{\pi}{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}\bigg).\end{split}

where we have used the definition of
\mathfrak{G}(\tau)
and for the first term we compute by (
6.19
)

1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi+r(\phi_{1})\delta\phi_{1}+\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|-\frac{r(\phi_{2})\delta\phi_{2}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}.

For
\phi\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}
, the RDE satisfied by
\delta\tilde{L}
is

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\phi}\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\cot(\bar{\alpha})\delta\tilde{L}(\phi)-\frac{\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}+S_{L},

with

\displaystyle S_{L}

\displaystyle=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\left(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\right)-\frac{1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}+\frac{\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\frac{\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg(-\cot(\bar{\alpha})^{2}\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}\Big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\Delta\phi\sin(\theta)\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=-\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi\big)\frac{(1-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))^{2}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{3}}+\sin(\theta)\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})-1}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}<0.

Hence the RDE for
\delta\tilde{r}-2.08\delta\tilde{L}
is

\frac{d}{d\phi}(\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi))>\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi))-\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

for
\phi\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}
. We can thus apply Lemma
3.14
and Remark
3.15
if we can show numerically that the function is positive and strictly increasing for
\tau\in[4,5]
.

Figure 48
.
Numerical plots of the function
\frac{\delta\rho(\tau)-e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau}\delta\tilde{L}(\tau)}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}
: the fist 5 rounds, the minimal values for
\theta=\frac{\pi}{2},\Delta\phi=0
, and its derivative for
\tau\in[4,5]
.

A numerical plot of the function
\delta\rho(\tau)-2.08e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau}\delta L(\tau)
,
\phi=\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}
and
(\theta,\Delta\phi)\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]\times[0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})]
, is in Fig.
48
: it is shown that the function is positive, increasing for
\tau\in[4,5]
and its minimal value: by Lemma
3.14
we conclude that

Proposition 6.17
.

It holds for the perturbation in the arc case

\delta\rho(\tau)-2.08e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau}\delta\tilde{L}(\tau)\geq 0.27e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau}\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi),\quad\tau\geq 1.

6.3.
An bound for the length of a spiral

We can put Propositions
6.8
and
6.17
together, obtaining the following

Corollary 6.18
.

Consider the spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;\phi_{0})
: if

\phi\geq\phi_{2}=\begin{cases}\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}+2\pi&\text{segment case},\\
\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}+2\pi&\text{arc case},\end{cases}

then

\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})\geq\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi)-2.08\tilde{L}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})>0.67e^{\bar{c}(\phi-\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{\pi}{2}+\bar{\alpha})},

Proof.

We can write

\displaystyle\tilde{r}(\phi)-2.08\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi)-2.08\tilde{L}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})+\int_{0}^{\phi_{0}}\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})-2.08\delta\tilde{L}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha};s_{0})ds_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi)-2.08\tilde{L}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}).\qed

7.
The optimal solution

In this section we construct the optimal candidate spiral for the minimization problem (
1.9
), (
4.1
). The previous section showed that the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{\zeta}(\phi;s_{0})
is optimal outside a small interval of rotation angle in the first round after
\phi_{0}
(for
\theta\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
): we thus construct a new family of spirals
\check{r}
whose structure takes into account that for some angles
\phi
it is better not to follow the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}
.

7.1.
Definition and construction of the optimal solution candidate

To describe the possible situations which can occur, depending on the relative position of
\phi_{0},\bar{\phi}
and the quantities
\bar{\theta}
,
\Delta\phi
(recall the definitions of the two quantities from Section
6
), we define the quantity

(7.1)

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{omegaopt}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\omega}}=\begin{cases}\bar{\theta}&\text{segment case},\\
\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi&\text{arc case},\end{cases}\qquad\text{so that}\ \bar{\theta}=\min\bigg\{\omega,\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg\},

and consider the following cases (see Figure
49
as a reference):

Point
(1)

\bar{\phi}-\phi_{0}\in[0,2\pi+\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}))
:

in this case we define

\hat{r}(\phi;s_{0})=\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0}),\quad\phi\in[\phi_{0},\bar{\phi}],

i.e. we follow the fastest saturated spiral, which we already know it is the optimal solution for the first round by Theorem
4.12
.

Point
(2)

\bar{\phi}-\phi_{0}-2\pi\in[\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}),\bar{\theta})
:

the solution
\hat{r}(\phi;s_{0})
will be a segment
[\check{P}_{0},\check{P}^{-}]
in the direction
\bar{\phi}-2\pi
. We stop at a length
\ell_{0}=|\check{P}^{-}-\check{P}_{0}|
such that the fastest saturated spiral starting at the end point
\check{P}^{-}
forms an initial angle whose value is on the boundary of the negativity region (it may happen that
\ell_{0}=0
). From this point onward, the solution is the fastest saturated spiral, which can start either with a segment or with a level set arc.

Point
(2^{\prime})

\bar{\phi}-\phi_{0}-2\pi\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\omega)
:

here we are in the arc case, and we follow the fastest saturated solution
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
in the interval

\phi\in\bigg[\phi_{0},\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg),

then we repeat the construction of the previous point: follow the direction
\bar{\phi}-2\pi
until we remain in the negativity region. Also in this case, after reaching the boundary of the negativity region, we close the last round starting either with a segment or with a level set arc.

Point
(3)

\bar{\phi}-\phi_{0}-2\pi-\omega\geq 0
:

we follow the fastest saturated solution
\tilde{\zeta}
up to the angle
\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}
, then we repeat Point (2) and Point
(2^{\prime})
above starting from a saturated point
\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}
. Note that when
\bar{\phi}=\phi_{0}+\omega+2\pi
, then

P_{2}=\tilde{\zeta}\bigg(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}\bigg),\quad P_{1}=\tilde{\zeta}(\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi),

and then
\bar{\phi}-2\pi\in\partial^{-}\tilde{\zeta}(\phi_{1})\cap\partial^{-}\tilde{\zeta}(\phi_{2})
, so that the two constructions above are the same. Here, we have indicated by
\phi_{1}=\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi
and by
\phi_{2}=\phi_{0}+\Delta\phi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}
as in Section
6.2
.

Figure 49
.
The three possible situations occurring in the construction of an element of the family, in the segment case (
\omega<\frac{\pi}{2}
). The blue curve represents the strategy
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
constructed in the previous section. In red the angle
\hat{\theta}
, representing the opening of the tent

We can summarize the construction as follows, Fig.
50
: we follow the fastest saturated solution
\tilde{r}
up to the angle
\check{\phi}_{0}
such that

(7.2)

\bar{\phi}\in\check{\phi}_{0}+2\pi+[\beta^{-}(\check{\phi}_{0}),\beta^{+}(\check{\phi}_{0})],

then a segment of length
\ell_{0}=|\check{P}^{-}-\check{P}_{0}|
determined by requiring that it is the last point such that the derivative of the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}^{\prime}
starting from that point satisfies
\delta\tilde{r}^{\prime}\leq 0
, and then the fastest saturated solution for the last round.

Figure 50
.
The construction of the optimal closing solution: the starting spiral is
\zeta
(black) up to the angle
\phi_{0}
. Then we follow the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{\zeta}
(blue) up to the angle
\check{\phi}_{0}
, determined by (
7.2
). At this point we follow the segment with direction
\bar{\phi}
until the angle between
\bar{\phi}
and the new fastest saturated spiral
\check{\zeta}
(red) is on the boundary of the negativity region. Note that while the blue spiral is inside the red one, the ray
\check{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})
is smaller than
\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})
.

Apriori, the perturbation of
\tilde{r}
in the last case (i.e. on the saturated part of
\tilde{r}
) can be either a segment of an arc, i.e. we are exiting the negativity region in the arc or segment case. This may only happen in the first round, because afterwards the last round corresponds to the segment case (Proposition
7.7
): due to the particular form of the solution in this case (two consecutive segments followed by a saturated spiral), we will call this construction a
tent
. In particular, Fig.
50
is correct when
\check{P}_{1}=\check{P}^{-}
, but its purpose was just for notation.

We next fix the notation for the optimal candidate solution, see Fig.
50
.

•

\bar{\phi}
is the final angle, where we want to minimize
r(\bar{\phi})
;

•

\phi_{0}
is the starting angle, with
\phi_{0}\leq\bar{\phi}
, from which the arc/segment of the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
starts;

•

the point
\check{P}_{0}
is the point of the spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
where a segment of length
\ell_{0}
starts with direction
\bar{\phi}
; the corresponding angle is
\check{\phi}_{0}
;

•

the segment of length
\ell_{0}
has endpoints
\check{P}_{0},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{P-check}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\check{P}^{-}}}
, and its direction belongs to the subdifferential to the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0}),\phi)
in the point
\check{P}_{0}
;

•

in the point
\check{P}^{-}
we compute the fastest saturated spiral: two cases again can occur (arc and segment, see however Proposition
7.7
);

•

if the segment case occurs in the point
\check{P}^{-}
we will denote it by
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{P1check}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\check{P}_{1}}}=\check{P}^{-}
;

•

if the arc case occurs in the point
\check{P}^{-}
we will denote by
\check{P}_{1}
the second endpoint of the arc of the level set of
u
, with opening
\Delta\check{\phi}
;

•

from the point
\check{P}_{1}
the spiral is a segment of length
\ell_{1}
and endpoints
\check{P}_{1},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{P2check}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\check{P}_{2}}}
;

•

the point
\check{P}_{2}
is saturated;

•

the exterior angle between the segments
[\check{P}_{0},\check{P}_{1}]
and
[\check{P}_{1},\check{P}_{2}]
is the critical angle
\hat{\theta}
in the segment case, while in the arc case the angle between
[\check{P}_{0},\check{P}^{-}]
and the level set arc is
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{h1Deltaphi}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}h_{1}(\Delta\phi)}}\leq\hat{\theta}
.

The first result of this section is that the above construction defines a unique spiral
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Given
s_{0}=s(\phi_{0})\geq 0
and an angle
\bar{\phi}\geq\phi_{0}
, there exists a unique spiral
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
with the properties above.

Proof.

The spiral
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
is defined once we know at what length
\ell_{0}
of the segment starting in
\check{P}_{0}
and pointing in the direction
\bar{\phi}
has to stop, and the spiral becomes the fastest saturated afterward. This point is when the derivative
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;\ell_{0})
of the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;\ell_{0})
starting from
\check{P}^{-}
,
|\check{P}^{-}-\check{P}_{0}|=\ell_{0}
, is
0
. Recall the definition of
\omega,\bar{\theta}
in (
7.1
).

Figure 51
.
The curves
\nu(\ell_{0})
of Proposition
7.1
in blue, and the boundary of the negativity region in red.

If we define
\omega(\ell_{0}),\theta(\ell_{0})
as the values of the angles
\omega
,
\theta
formed by the fastest saturated spiral along the segment
\ell_{0}e^{\imath\bar{\phi}}
(recall that
\theta
is the angle
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{thetatildeseg}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{\theta}}}-\theta_{0}
, where
\theta_{0}=\beta^{-}(\check{P}_{0})
is the direction of the perturbation), we have defined a curve

\mathbb{R}^{+}\ni\ell_{0}\mapsto\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{nuell0}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\nu(\ell_{0})}}=(\theta(\ell_{0}),\omega(\ell_{0}))\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

The statement is proved if we show that this curve crosses the boundary of the negativity region
N=N_{\mathrm{segm}}\cup N_{\mathrm{arc}}
(Propositions
6.15
and
6.7
) only in one point: if this happens, the crossing point is a minimum for the quantity
\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi};\ell_{0})
, see Fig.
51
.

In the case the initial value is already outside the negativity region (i.e.
\theta>h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
), then we deduce that
\ell_{0}=0
. This could happen for example in the case
\theta>\hat{\theta}
, as for instance in the second picture of Figure
49
, where the angle
\theta
is sufficiently big. In this case
\ell_{0}=0
and the optimal solution coincides with the fastest saturated spiral.

We observe that after the point
P_{0}
, the segment
[\check{P}_{0},\check{P}^{-}]
starts tangent to the spiral
\tilde{\zeta}
, i.e.
\theta=0
: thus we always start in the negativity region of
\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})
(i.e.
\ell_{0}>0
) apart from the case where
\check{P}_{0}=P_{0}
. Summarizing, we encounter two different situations,
P_{0}=\check{P}_{0}
and
P_{0}\not=\check{P}_{0}
. In the first case we compute
\theta
, and if
\theta>\hat{\theta}
then
\ell_{0}=0
and the optimal solution is the fastest saturated spiral (if it is not, we find
\ell_{0}
such that the angle formed with the solution is exactly
\hat{\theta}
). In the second situation, since we start tangent to the fastest saturated spiral in the point
\check{P}_{0}
, then
\theta=0
and we are in the negativity region. Again,
\ell_{0}
is such that the fastest saturated spiral computed at the endpoint
\check{P}_{1}
forms exactly an angle
\hat{\theta}
in the segment case and
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
in the arc case.

We can compute the derivative of the curve
\nu(\ell_{0})
by using the formulas of the previous section.

Segment case:

we have from (
6.1
)

\frac{d\bar{\theta}}{d\ell_{0}}=\frac{1}{|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|}\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta))\big)-\frac{\sin(\check{\theta}_{1})}{\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{1})},

\frac{d\theta}{d\ell_{0}}=\frac{1}{|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|}\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big(1-\cos(\theta)\big)\big),

where we have used Equation (
6.1
) and where
\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{1})
is the length of the segment part of the optimal ray ending in the initial point
\check{P}_{1}
and
\check{\theta}_{1}=\beta^{-}(\check{\phi}_{1})=\beta^{+}(\check{\phi}_{1})-\theta
. See Figure
52
as a reference. We point out that the derivative is computed in the point
\check{P}_{1}
.

Figure 52
.
The situation of Proposition
7.1
in the segment case.

Hence, since
\frac{d\theta}{d\ell_{0}}>0
unless
\theta=0
, the curve
\nu(\ell_{0})
which in the segment case is
(\theta(\ell_{0}),\bar{\theta}(\ell_{0}))
can be represented as the graph of a differentiable function
\bar{\theta}(\theta)
, parameterized with
\theta
increasing. Since in the segment case the boundary of the negativity region is
\theta=\hat{\theta}
, this curve exits the negativity region only in one point, corresponding to a unique value
\ell_{0}
.

In the case
\check{P}_{0}=\check{P}_{1}=\check{P}_{2}
one can check by a local expansion about a saturated point that

\frac{d\theta}{d\ell_{0}}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{0})},\quad\frac{d\bar{\theta}}{d\ell_{0}}=0.

Hence the curve
\nu(0)
starts with direction
(0,1)
.

Arc case:

here we use (
6.20
) and the fact that the angle of the level set with the ray is
\frac{\pi}{2}
to write

\frac{d\omega}{d\ell_{0}}=\frac{d\Delta\check{\phi}}{d\ell_{0}}=\frac{1}{|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|}\big(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta))+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)\Delta\check{\phi}\big)-\frac{\cos(\theta)}{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})},

\frac{d\theta}{d\ell_{0}}=\frac{\cos(\theta)}{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})},

with
\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})
the segment part of the optimal ray ending in
\check{P}^{-}
. Hence the curve can be represented again as the graph of a function
\Delta\check{\phi}(\theta)
, oriented along the increasing
\theta
axis, and such that

\frac{d\Delta\check{\phi}}{d\theta}=\frac{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})}{|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|}\frac{\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)}{\cos(\theta)}-1.

The computation of Lemma
6.16
gives that the derivative of the boundary curve
h_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi})
is (see Equation (
6.26
))

\bigg(\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\check{\phi}}\bigg)^{-1}=-1-\frac{\cos(\theta)\Delta\check{\phi}}{\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\check{\phi})},

and then it would follow that the curves are not transversal only if

\frac{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})}{|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|}=-\frac{\cos(\theta)^{2}\Delta\check{\phi}}{(\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\check{\phi}))^{2}}\leq 0,

which is impossible.

Thus the curves are transversal, and the crossing occurs from the negative region to the positive region: indeed this is clear in the segment case, while in the arc case

\text{if}\quad\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\check{\phi})<0\quad\text{then}\quad\frac{d\Delta\check{\phi}}{d\theta}<-1<\bigg(\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\check{\phi}}\bigg)^{-1},

\text{if}\quad\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\check{\phi})>0\quad\text{then}\quad\frac{d\Delta\check{\phi}}{d\theta}>-1>\bigg(\frac{dh_{1}}{d\Delta\check{\phi}}\bigg)^{-1},

meaning that if you cross the negative region, then the crossing occurs always with the same direction.
The above inequalities yield the statement.
∎

Corollary 7.2
.

Assume that along the line
\check{P}_{0}+\mathbb{R}^{+}e^{i\bar{\phi}}
we find a point
\check{P}^{-}
such that the fastest saturated spiral starting from
\check{P}^{-}
form the critical angle
\theta=\hat{\theta}
in the segment case (i.e.
\check{P}^{-}=\check{P}_{1}
) or
\theta=h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
in the arc case. Then this is the starting point of the unique spiral of Proposition
7.1
.

Proof.

Indeed, the map
\ell_{0}\to(\theta,\omega)
is a smooth curve strictly increasing in
\theta
and crossing the line of critical angle
\bar{\theta}
(segment case) or
h_{1}(\Delta\phi)
(arc case) exactly in one point.
∎

In particular, the segment case is admissible if and only if the tip of the tent
\check{P}_{1}
is such that the angle of the optimal ray ending in
\check{P}_{1}
and the segment
\ell_{1}
is
\leq\frac{\pi}{2}
: this is the criterion we will check in Section
7.2.1
.

Remark 7.3
.

Observe that along the line
\Delta\check{\phi}=0
, i.e.
\bar{\theta}=\frac{\pi}{2}
and
\check{\theta}_{1}=\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta
, we have

\frac{d\omega}{d\theta}=\frac{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})}{|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|}\frac{\sin\theta-\cot\bar{\alpha}(1-\cos\theta)}{\cos\theta}-1.

We want to check whether from the arc case (meaning that the fastest saturated spiral starts with an arc in the point
\check{P}^{-}
) there is a transition from the segment solution.
By observing that the spirals are convex and the angle at the top of the segment
\ell_{0}
with the optimal ray is
\bar{\alpha}
, by elementary geometrical reasons we must have

|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|\tan\bar{\alpha}=\ell_{1}\tan\bar{\alpha}\leq\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{-}),

in particular the previous inequality becomes

\frac{d\omega}{d\theta}>\tan(\bar{\alpha})\frac{\sin(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})(1-\cos(\theta))}{\cos(\theta)}-1=\frac{\tan(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta)-1}{\cos(\theta)}.

This quantity is positive whenever
\theta\geq\sin^{-1}(\cot(\bar{\alpha}))=0.426813
. In particular, in this range there is no transition from arc solutions to segment solutions.

The previous proposition proves that construction of the spiral
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
at the beginning of the section is well defined. To prove that it is the minimizer for the minimization problem (
4.1
), we need to study the positivity of
\check{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})
w.r.t. to perturbations. More precisely, we want to compute the derivative

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{deltarcheck}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\delta\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})}}=\frac{d}{ds_{0}}\check{r}(\phi;s_{0}).

Similarly to the computation of
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
, depending on the structure of the solution
\check{r}
several cases are possible: the initial fastest saturated part of
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
can be a segment or a level-set arc, and the segment starting in
\check{P}_{0}
with length
\ell_{0}
may be followed by a segment (tent case) or an arc.

The most important case is when the last round starts with segment (tent case), which we analyze in the next section.

7.2.
Formulas for the tent case

Figure 53
.
The geometry used to obtain the formulas for the tent case, Section
7.2
.

In order to study the correction to the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}
after the angle
\check{\phi}_{0}
, we need to study some geometric relations among the length
\ell_{0}
of this segment and the underlying spiral
\tilde{r}
. This section is thus devoted to obtain these formulas, depending on the following quantities, which in order to distinguish from the previous ones are denoted with a hat, see Fig.
53
:

•

the entrance ray
\hat{r}_{0}
at angle
0
initial position
\hat{Q}_{0}
, the end point
\hat{P}_{0}
of
\hat{r}_{0}
is saturated;

•

the exit ray
\hat{r}_{2}
at angle
\hat{\theta}
with initial position
\hat{Q}_{2}
, the end point of
\hat{r}_{2}
is saturated;

•

the length of segments
\hat{\ell}_{0}
,
\hat{\ell}_{1}
;

•

the length
\hat{L}
of the spiral at the base between the points
\hat{Q}_{0},\hat{Q}_{2}
(angle opening
[0,\hat{\theta}]
).

We will also give conditions to verify that it is admissible, which is equivalent to check that the angle formed by segment
[\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Qhat1tent}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{Q}_{1}}},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Phat1tent}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{P}_{1}}}]
and the second segment
[\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Phat1tent}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{P}_{1}}},\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Phat2tent}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{P}_{2}}}]
is
\angle\hat{Q}_{1}\hat{P}_{1}\hat{P}_{2}\geq\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Since
\hat{P}_{0}
is saturated, the saturation condition at the end point of the segment
\ell_{1}
reads as

(7.3)

\hat{r}_{2}+\hat{L}-\hat{r}_{0}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\ell}_{0}+\hat{\ell}_{1})=0,

and the vector relation among the various points is

(7.4)

\hat{Q}_{0}+\hat{r}_{0}(1,0)+\hat{\ell}_{0}e^{\imath\bar{\alpha}}+\hat{\ell}_{1}e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})}=\hat{Q}_{2}+\hat{r}_{2}e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}.

Projecting the second equation in the direction
e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}
we obtain

(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}+\hat{r}_{2}-\hat{\ell}_{1}\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\hat{\ell}_{0}\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\hat{r}_{0}\cos(\hat{\theta})=0,

and then using the saturation condition

(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}-\hat{\ell}_{0}\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\hat{r}_{0}\cos(\hat{\theta})=\hat{L}-\hat{r}_{0}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\hat{\ell}_{0},

\begin{split}\hat{\ell}_{0}&=\frac{(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}-\hat{L}+\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}.\end{split}

Projecting again (
7.4
) on the direction
e^{\imath(\alpha+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}

(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}+\hat{r}_{2}\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\hat{\ell}_{0}\sin(\hat{\theta})-\hat{r}_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})=0,

which gives

\hat{r}_{2}=\frac{\hat{r}_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})+\hat{\ell}_{0}\sin(\hat{\theta})-(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}.

We can now compute
\hat{\ell}_{1}
from (
7.3
) as

\hat{\ell}_{1}=\frac{\hat{r}_{2}+\hat{L}-\hat{r}_{0}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\hat{\ell}_{0}}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}.

Finally, the relation between
\hat{L}
and
\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0}
is given by integrating the curve
\hat{\zeta}
:

\frac{d\hat{\zeta}}{d\theta}=e^{\imath\theta}\hat{R}(\theta),\quad\hat{\zeta}(\theta)-\hat{\zeta}(\theta_{0})=\int_{\theta_{0}}^{\theta}e^{\imath\omega}\hat{R}(\omega)d\omega,

where
\hat{R}
is the radius of curvature of
\hat{\zeta}
. The above formula holds also when the curvature is
\infty
, by considering
ds=\hat{R}d\theta
as a measure.

We summarize the results obtained in the following lemma.

Lemma 7.4
.

Consider the geometric configuration as in Fig.
53
, with
\hat{P}_{0},\hat{P}_{2}
saturated. Then the following geometric relations hold:

\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0}=\int_{0}^{\hat{\theta}}e^{\imath\omega}\hat{R}(\omega)d\omega,\quad\hat{R}(\theta)\ \text{curvature of }\ \hat{\zeta},

\hat{L}=\int_{0}^{\hat{\theta}}\hat{R}(\theta)d\theta,

(7.5)

\hat{\ell}_{0}=\frac{(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}-\hat{L}+\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})},

\hat{r}_{2}=\frac{\hat{r}_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})+\hat{\ell}_{0}\sin(\hat{\theta})-(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})},

\hat{\ell}_{1}=\frac{\hat{r}_{2}+\hat{L}-\hat{r}_{0}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\hat{\ell}_{0}}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}.

Since all formulas are linear, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 7.5
.

The same formulas of Lemma
7.4
hold for the perturbation
\delta\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
.

For future use, define the shift
\hat{\tau}
in the
\tau
-coordinate corresponding to the angle shift
\hat{\theta}
:

(7.6)

\hat{\tau}=\frac{\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}.

7.2.1.
Estimates for the admissibility of the tent

The next series of lemmas give sufficient conditions which ensure that the construction above is admissible, i.e. the angle between the segments
[\hat{Q}_{1},\hat{P}_{1}]
and
[\hat{P}_{1},\hat{P}_{2}]
is
\angle\hat{Q}_{1}\hat{P}_{1}\hat{P}_{2}\geq\frac{\pi}{2}
. If
\hat{Q}_{1}=\hat{\zeta}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{phihat}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{\phi}}})
, then we must require

\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}<\frac{\pi}{2},\quad\hat{\phi}\geq 0.113642.

Lemma 7.6
.

Assume that we are in the configuration of Fig.
53

with
\hat{P}_{0}
and
\hat{P}_{2}
saturated
: if

\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\leq\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Kadmis}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}K_{\mathrm{tent}}}}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})(\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))}{\cos(\hat{\theta})(1-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))}<0.966795,

then the tent is admissible.

Proof.

We have to study the minimum of the function
\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}
in order to have that
\hat{P}_{1}
belongs to the critical line
\hat{Q}_{1}+\mathbb{R}^{+}e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}
: we will show that in order to have this, we must have
\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\geq K_{\mathrm{tent}}
.

If
\hat{Q}_{1}+\mathbb{R}^{+}e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}
, we must satisfy the vector relation

\hat{Q}_{0}+\hat{r}_{0}e^{\imath 0}+\hat{\ell}_{0}e^{i\bar{\alpha}}=\hat{P}_{1},

with by (
7.5
)

\hat{\ell}_{0}\big(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\big)=(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}-\hat{L}+\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta})).

We can fix the point
\hat{Q}_{1}
, and observe that the geometric configuration with minimal length is when
\hat{Q}_{2}\in\hat{Q}_{1}+\mathbb{R}^{+}e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}
: indeed in the formula for
\hat{\ell}_{0}
we can write

(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath\hat{\theta}}-\hat{L}=\int_{0}^{\hat{\theta}}(\cos(\hat{\theta}-\theta)-1)d\hat{L}(\theta)=\bigg[\int_{0}^{\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}}+\int_{\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\hat{\theta}}\bigg](\cos(\hat{\theta}-\theta)-1)d\hat{L}(\theta).

In the second integral one can reduce the quantity

\int_{\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}}^{\hat{\theta}}d\hat{L}(\theta)

if we take the maximal value of
1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\theta)
, which is when
\theta=\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}
. This means that we can assume
\hat{Q}_{2}\in\hat{Q}_{0}+\mathbb{R}^{+}e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}
.

The saturation gives then

\hat{L}+|\hat{P}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{2}|-\hat{r}_{0}=\hat{L}+\frac{|\hat{P}_{1}-\hat{Q}_{2}|}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\hat{r}_{0}=\cos(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\ell}_{0}+\hat{\ell}_{1})=\cos(\bar{\alpha})\big(\hat{\ell}_{0}+\cot(\bar{\alpha})|\hat{P}_{1}-\hat{Q}_{2}|\big),

which is

\hat{L}+\sin(\bar{\alpha})|\hat{P}_{1}-\hat{Q}_{2}|-\hat{r}_{0}=\cos(\bar{\alpha})\hat{\ell}_{0}.

A configuration for which the quantity
K_{\mathrm{tent}}
of the statement is reached is in Fig.
54(a)
. For this configuration, elementary geometry gives

\hat{r}_{0}\frac{\sin(\hat{\phi})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\phi})}=\hat{\ell}_{0}=\frac{\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))-\hat{L}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}))}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})},

where we have used Equation (
7.5
), i.e.

\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\phi})(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))-\sin(\hat{\phi})(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))}{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\phi})(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}))}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})(\cos(\hat{\phi})-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}))}{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\phi})(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}))}.

The function

(7.7)

\hat{\phi}\mapsto\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})(\cos(\hat{\phi})-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}))}{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\phi})(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\hat{\phi}))}

is such that if
\hat{L}_{0}/\hat{r}_{0}<0.966795
, then the angle corresponding to
\hat{P}_{1}
is larger than
\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}
, see Fig.
54(b)
.

(a)

The worst scenario of Lemma
7.6
.

(b)

Plot of the function (
7.7
) in blue, in orange the value of
\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}
for the critical angle
\hat{\phi}=\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}
.

(c)

The geometry of the perturbation used to estimate the tent in the general case.

Figure 54
.
Analysis of Lemma
7.6
.

For a generic configuration, we consider a variation of the geometry as follows (see Fig.
54(c)
): keeping
\hat{Q}_{1}
,
\hat{P}_{1}
fixed, we move backward the point
\hat{Q}_{0}
along a convex curve and vary
\hat{r}_{0},\hat{\ell}_{0}
accordingly in order to preserve their directions. In particular, if
\theta
is the direction of the tangent of the convex curve in
\hat{Q}_{0}
, we obtain from the vector relations

\delta\hat{\ell}_{0}=\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\frac{\sin(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})},\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\cos(\theta)-\delta\hat{\ell}_{0}\cos(\bar{\alpha})=\delta\hat{Q}_{0}(\cos(\theta)-\sin(\theta)\cot(\bar{\alpha})).

Plugging this relation into the formula for
\hat{\ell}_{0}
we obtain

\delta\hat{\ell}_{0}(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))=\delta\hat{Q}_{0}(-1+\cos(\hat{\theta}-\theta))+\delta\hat{Q}_{2}(1-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))+\delta\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta})),

because
\hat{Q}_{2}
is assumed to move along the curve
\hat{Q}_{1}+\mathbb{R}^{+}e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}
towards
\hat{Q}_{1}
. Substituting we obtain

\begin{split}\delta\hat{Q}_{2}(1-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))&=\delta\hat{\ell}_{0}(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))+\delta\hat{Q}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\theta))-\delta\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))\\
&=\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\bigg[\frac{\sin(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))+(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}-\theta))-(\cos(\theta)-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\sin(\theta))(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))\bigg]\\
&=\delta\hat{Q}_{0}(1-\cos(\theta)).\end{split}

Hence we have

\frac{\delta\hat{L}}{\delta\hat{r}_{0}}=\frac{\delta\hat{Q}_{0}-\delta\hat{Q}_{2}}{\delta\hat{r}_{0}}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})(\cos(\theta)-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))}{(1-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)}.

Note that this formula coincides with
K_{\mathrm{tent}}
for
\theta=\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}
. By taking of the r.h.s. we have that

\frac{d}{d\theta}\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})(\cos(\theta)-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))}{(1-\sin(\bar{\alpha}))\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)}=\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{1-\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\theta)^{2}}

whose maximum in the interval
\theta\in[0,\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}]
is for
\theta=\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}
and equal to

\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{1-\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\sin(\hat{\theta})\sin(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\hat{\theta})^{2}}=-1.03949<0.

Hence

\delta\bigg(\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}-K_{\mathrm{tent}}\bigg)=\frac{\delta\hat{r}_{0}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\bigg(\frac{\delta\hat{L}}{\delta\hat{r}_{0}}-\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\bigg)>\frac{\delta\hat{r}_{0}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\bigg(K_{\mathrm{tent}}-\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\bigg)\quad\text{for}\ \theta<\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2},

and then we conclude that

\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}-K_{\mathrm{tent}}>0

unless we are in the critical configuration where
\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}-K_{\mathrm{tent}}=0
.

We conclude that is
\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}<K_{\mathrm{tent}}
then the angle
\hat{\zeta}(\hat{\phi})=\hat{Q}_{1}
is larger than the critical angle
\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}
.
∎

We have proved that, under certain conditions on the length
\hat{L}
and on
\hat{r}_{0}
, the tent is admissible, meaning that
\angle\hat{Q}_{1}\hat{P}_{1}\hat{P}_{2}\geq\frac{\pi}{2}
. In particular, if
\frac{\hat{L}}{\hat{r}_{0}}\geq 0.966795..
, then the fasted saturated spiral computed at
\hat{P}_{1}
starts with an arc.

We next prove that in such a case (i.e. when we start with an arc), after one round the functional
r(\phi)-2.08L(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})
will be still negative. This is a consequence of the fact that
\hat{r}_{0}-\hat{L}\sim 0.04
, so that we need
\hat{L}
to be almost of the size of
\hat{r}_{0}
, and we need at least one round to make
r(\phi)
more than twice the length
L(\phi-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})
.

Consider indeed the spiral
\tilde{r}(\phi;\phi_{0})
, and let
\hat{\phi}_{0}
be the initial angle for the tent and
\hat{\phi}_{2}=\hat{\phi}_{0}+\hat{\theta}
the final angle, and let
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{phihat3}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\hat{\phi}_{3}}}\geq\hat{\phi}_{0}
an angle where we are going to estimate the functional
r(\cdot)-2.08L(\cdot-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})
. By considering the fastest growing spiral

\dot{r}=\cot(\bar{\alpha})r,

and observing that the best situation is the one of the previous lemma (because the length
\hat{L}
is the minimal and is applied at the latest possible angle), we obtain that if the tent is not admissible and
\hat{\phi}_{3}\geq\hat{\phi}_{2}

\begin{split}\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{3})&\leq\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{0})e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\phi}_{3}-\hat{\phi}_{0})}-(\tilde{L}(\hat{\phi}_{0}+\hat{\theta})-\tilde{L}(\hat{\phi}_{0}))e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\phi}_{3}-\hat{\phi}_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}\\
&\leq(\tilde{L}(\hat{\phi}_{0}+\hat{\theta})-\tilde{L}(\hat{\phi}_{0}))\bigg(\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{K_{\mathrm{tent}}}-1\bigg)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\phi}_{3}-\hat{\phi}_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}\\
&\leq\tilde{L}(\hat{\phi}_{3}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})\bigg(\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{K_{\mathrm{tent}}}-1\bigg)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\phi}_{3}-\hat{\phi}_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}.\end{split}

If now

\hat{\phi}_{3}>\phi_{0}+\omega+2\pi,

we must have that Corollary
6.18
is valid, i.e.

\bigg(\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{K_{\mathrm{tent}}}-1\bigg)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\hat{\phi}_{3}-\hat{\phi}_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}))}\geq 2.08.

Numerically one obtains

\hat{\phi}_{3}-\hat{\phi}_{0}\geq\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})\ln\bigg(\frac{2.08}{\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{K_{\mathrm{tent}}}-1}\bigg)=7.86127,

which in the
\tau
coordinates corresponds to

(7.8)

\hat{\tau}_{3}-\hat{\tau}_{0}\geq 1.05358.

We thus have the following

Proposition 7.7
.

The tent is admissible for if the starting angle
\hat{\phi}_{0}
is larger that
\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}
(segment case) of
\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi-\bar{\alpha}
.

Proof.

The initial angle corresponds to
\tau=0
in the
\tau
-coordinates, and Corollary
6.18
must be true at
\tau=1
:

Hence if the tent is not admissible for
\tau>0
, there is a
\tau>1
such that Corollary
6.18
is not true by (
7.8
), which is a contradiction. ∎

Remark 7.8
.

It is easy to see that the tent is not always admissible: if we assume that the spiral
\zeta
has a very long segment,
\zeta
asymptotically becomes by scaling

\Omega_{0}=B_{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}(0),\quad\zeta=[0,1],

for which the fastest saturated is an arc. Indeed, in this situation the saturation condition for the tent reads as

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\hat{\ell}_{1}\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\ell}_{0}\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})+\cos(\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})(1+\hat{\ell}_{0}+\hat{\ell}_{1})

\displaystyle=\hat{\ell}_{0}(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))-2\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\cos(\hat{\theta}).

Being

2\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\hat{\theta})=-0.10964<0,

the tent is not admissible.

7.2.2.
An estimate on the final value
\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})

To conclude this section, we give a simplified formula for estimating the final value of
\hat{r}
and the angle
\bar{\phi}=\hat{\phi}_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\alpha}
.

Lemma 7.9
.

Assume that base of the tent is a convex curve,
\hat{P}_{0}
is saturated and that
\tilde{r}
is the saturated spiral starting from
\hat{\phi}_{0}
tangent to the tent in the point
\hat{P}_{0}
. Then it holds

\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\hat{\ell}_{0}\geq\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\hat{r}_{0}.

Proof.

Since the base of the spiral is a convex curve, then

|\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0}|\leq\hat{L},

and then by Equation (
7.5
)

\hat{\ell}_{0}\leq\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\hat{r}_{0}.

By Theorem
4.12
it holds also

\hat{r}_{2}\geq\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{2})=\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{0}+\hat{\theta}).

Since
\hat{r}
and
r
satisfy after the angle
\hat{\phi}_{2}
the same RDE, their difference satisfies

\frac{d}{d\theta}(\hat{r}(\phi)-r(\phi))=\cot\bar{\alpha}(\hat{r}(\phi)-r(\phi)).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})

\displaystyle=(\hat{r}_{2}-\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{2}))e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}+\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\hat{\ell}_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\hat{r}_{0}.\qed

The last computation of the above proof will be used several times in the next sections:

(7.9)

\displaystyle\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})

\displaystyle=(\hat{r}_{2}-\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{2}))e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}+\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\hat{\ell}_{0}.

8.
Optimal solution candidate

In this section we construct the optimal candidate
r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\phi)
when the initial burning set is
\{0\}
and the spiral is constructed outside
B_{1}(0)
. We will show the structure of the optimal solution candidate depending on the final angle
\bar{\phi}
and its asymptotic behavior.

8.1.
Optimal solution candidate for
\bar{\phi}\in[0,2\pi)
: see Fig.
24

In this case the optimal solution is the fastest saturated spiral
r_{\mathrm{opt}}=\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}
, according to Theorem
4.12
, and by Theorem
5.1
the solution is positive.

8.2.
Optimal solution candidate for
\bar{\phi}\in[2\pi,2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2})
: see Fig.
55(a)

(a)

Geometric situation of Section
8.2
.

(b)

Numerical plot of the function
r_{\mathrm{opt}}
(orange) and
S_{\mathtt{a}}
with
\mathtt{a}=0
(blue).

Figure 55
.
Analysis of Section
8.2
.

In this case we move along the direction
\bar{\phi}
until we are in the critical curve
\check{\theta}=h_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi})
: the saturation condition at the point
\check{P}_{2}
is

\frac{\check{\ell}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\big(h+\check{\ell}\Delta\check{\phi}+\check{\ell}\cot(\bar{\alpha})\big)=0,\quad\check{\ell}=\sqrt{1+h^{2}+2h\cos(\bar{\phi})},\quad\sin(\check{\phi})=\frac{h}{\check{\ell}}\sin(\bar{\phi}),

which gives

(8.1)

\Delta\check{\phi}=\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{h}{\check{\ell}}.

The final value is then

(8.2)

r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi},h)=\frac{\check{\ell}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-(\check{\phi}+\Delta\check{\phi}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}))}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2\pi)}-h.

A plot is in Fig.
55(b)
, where the function
e^{-\bar{c}\phi}r_{\mathrm{opt}}
and the fastest saturated spiral
e^{-\bar{c}\phi}\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}
of Section
5
are plotted together: the figure presents only the part where
r_{\mathrm{opt}}
is below
\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}
. Note that the region where it is convenient to use
r_{\mathrm{opt}}
is for

\bar{\phi}>2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}-h_{1}(\tan(\bar{\alpha}))=7.73645,

as Proposition
6.15
requires. The optimal value is computed by imposing
h=h_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi})
together with (
8.1
).

In order to verify that the tent is not an admissible solution, one could check that
\check{\Delta}\phi>0
or try to compute the geometric quantities for a tent: indeed, if we try to replace the arc with the tent and we use the saturation condition and the optimality condition
\theta=\hat{\theta}
of Proposition
7.1
, we obtain that if
P_{2}
is the first saturated point of the fastest closing spiral starting in
P_{1}=(1+h\cos(\bar{\phi}),h\sin(\bar{\phi}))
, then from geometric consideration and the saturation condition

|P_{2}|=\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\bar{\phi}+2\pi)+h\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})+\ell_{1}\cos(\bar{\alpha}),\quad|P_{2}|-\cos(\bar{\alpha})(h+\ell_{1})=0,

where
\ell_{1}=|P_{2}-P_{1}|
, so that

h=-\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\bar{\phi}+2\pi)}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}<0

for
\phi\in[2\pi,2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}]
, so it cannot be an admissible solution.

We summarize these results into the following lemma.

Lemma 8.1
.

If
\bar{\phi}\in 2\pi+[0,\frac{\pi}{2}-h_{1}(\tan(\bar{\alpha}))]
, the optimal solution is the saturated spiral. For
\bar{\phi}\in 2\pi+[\frac{\pi}{2}-h_{1}(\tan(\bar{\alpha})),\frac{\pi}{2}]
the optimal solution is a segment in the direction
\bar{\phi}
followed by an arc. The solution remains positive.

8.3.
Optimal solution candidate for
\bar{\phi}\in[2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2},2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))
: see Fig.
56(a)
,
56(b)

(a)

Geometric situation of Section
8.3
in the arc case.

(b)

Geometric situation of Section
8.3
in the tent case.

Figure 56
.
Analysis of Section
8.3
.

In this case the solution
r_{\mathrm{opt}}
switches from the arc to the tent as the angle
\bar{\phi}
increases: indeed from Proposition
7.7
we know that in the saturated region the tent is the admissible solution. For the arc case (Figure
56(a)
), the saturation condition at the point
\check{P}_{2}
is

\frac{\check{\ell}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}+h+\check{\ell}\Delta\check{\phi}+\check{\ell}\cot(\bar{\alpha})\bigg)=0,\quad\check{\ell}=\sqrt{1+h^{2}},\quad\sin(\check{\phi})=\frac{h}{\check{\ell}},

i.e.

(8.3)

\Delta\check{\phi}=\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}+h}{\check{\ell}}.

which is positive for (see Fig.
57(a)
)

\bar{\phi}\leq 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})\check{\ell}-h.

).

).

.

.

) multiplied by

.

8.3
.

In the region (see Fig.
57(b)
)

(8.4)

\bar{\phi}>2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})\check{\ell}-h

the arc is not admissible having
\Delta\check{\phi}<0
, so that the solution will be the tent. The final value in the arc case is

(8.5)

\begin{split}r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi})&=\frac{\check{\ell}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\check{\phi}+\check{\phi}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}))}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2\pi)}-\int_{0}^{\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}}e^{\cot{\bar{\alpha}}\phi^{\prime}}d\phi^{\prime}-h\\
&=\frac{\check{\ell}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-(\check{\phi}+\Delta\check{\phi}))}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2\pi)}-\frac{e^{\cot{\bar{\alpha}}(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2})}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}-h.\end{split}

which is positive
\geq 1.6e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-(\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})))}
also in the region where
\Delta\check{\phi}<0
, see Fig.
57(c)
.

We next compute the tent case with final angle
\hat{\theta}
, which we know to be the optimal angle at the end point of the segment
h
: the saturation gives here

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\big(\ell_{1}\cos(\bar{\alpha})+h\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})\big)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}+h+\ell_{1}\bigg)

\displaystyle=h(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))+\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg),

(8.6)

h=\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2})-\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}.

This quantity is positive only for (see Fig.
57(d)
)

\displaystyle\bar{\phi}

\displaystyle\geq 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}=2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}=2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})-.787658=9.48195.

The angle between the ray departing from the origin and arriving at the end of the segment
h
and the segment with angle
\hat{\theta}
is

\pi-\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}+\arctan(h)=\frac{\pi}{2}+\arctan(h)-\hat{\theta},

so that the requirement to be
\geq\frac{\pi}{2}
for admissibility gives

(8.7)

h\geq\tan(\hat{\theta})=.554249,\quad\bar{\phi}\geq 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\sin(\hat{\theta})\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})\cos(\hat{\theta})}=10.0161.

The point
(h,\bar{\phi})=(.554249,10.0161)
belongs to the curve where
\Delta\check{\phi}=0
, and from that point onward the value
h
given by Equation (
8.5
) is inside the region where
\Delta\check{\phi}<0
, see Fig.
57(e)
.

The final value of the solution for the tent case in the admissibility region is

(8.8)

r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi})=\bigg(\frac{\cos(\arctan(h)-\hat{\theta})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\sqrt{1+h^{2}}\bigg)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2\pi)}-\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2})}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}-h,

with
h
given by (
8.6
). A numerical plot is in Fig.
57(f)
.

We summarize the results in the following lemma.

Lemma 8.2
.

If
\bar{\phi}\in 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+[0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}]
, the optimal solution is a segment in the direction
\bar{\phi}
followed by an arc. If
\bar{\phi}\in 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+[\tan(\bar{\alpha})-\frac{\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})},\tan(\bar{\alpha})]
, the optimal solution is the tent. Both solutions remain positive.

8.4.
Optimal solution candidate for
\bar{\phi}\geq 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha})
: see Fig.
58(a)

(a)

The optimal candidate
r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\phi)
for
\bar{\phi}\geq 2\pi+\tan(\bar{\alpha})+\frac{\pi}{2}
.

(b)

Numerical plot of the functional (
8.9
).

Figure 58
.
Analysis of Section
8.4
.

We first observe that by Proposition
7.7
the tent is admissible.

Next we have to apply the formulas of Lemma
7.4
in the saturated part, in order to compute the final value
r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi})
. Using Lemma
7.9
, we need to estimate the function

(8.9)

\rho(\bar{\tau})-\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\rho(\bar{\tau}-1),\quad\bar{\tau}\geq 1.

A numerical plot is in Fig.
58(b)
: we observe that it is positive and increasing for
\tau\in[4,5]
: then by Lemma
3.14
we conclude that

Proposition 8.3
.

For
\bar{\tau}\geq 1
, the optimal solution
r_{\mathrm{opt}}
is made by a final tent with initial point in
\tau-1
and moreover
r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi})>0
.

8.5.
Asymptotic behavior of the optimal candidate

We can compute the asymptotic value of the optimal solution, using the asymptotic value of the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi)
given by Theorem
5.1
for
\bar{\phi}\gg 1
:

\displaystyle\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\phi)

\displaystyle\sim 2\phi e^{\bar{c}\phi}\bigg(\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(\tan(\bar{\alpha})+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}-e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi)}-\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2})}-e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}}{\bar{c}}-\cot(\bar{\alpha})e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\tan(\bar{\alpha}))}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Kasymptosat}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}K_{\mathrm{asympt}}}}\phi e^{\bar{c}\phi}.

with
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Kasymptosat}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}K_{\mathrm{asympt}}}}=0.149681
.

One thus obtain the estimates for the tent computations of Lemma
7.4

\displaystyle\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0}

\displaystyle=e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}\int_{0}^{\hat{\theta}}e^{\imath\omega}\frac{\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\omega+(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})))}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}d\omega

\displaystyle\sim K_{\mathrm{asympt}}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\int_{0}^{\hat{\theta}}e^{\imath\omega}e^{\bar{c}\omega}d\omega

\displaystyle=K_{\mathrm{asympt}}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{e^{(\bar{c}+\imath)\hat{\theta}}-1}{\bar{c}+\imath},

\displaystyle(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}

\displaystyle\sim K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\frac{\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\bar{c}(e^{\bar{c}\hat{\theta}}-\cos(\hat{\theta}))+\sin(\hat{\theta})}{1+\bar{c}^{2}}

\displaystyle=0.564562K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))},

\displaystyle(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}

\displaystyle\sim K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\frac{\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{\bar{c}(e^{\bar{c}\hat{\theta}}\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}))+(e^{\bar{c}\hat{\theta}}\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}))}{1+\bar{c}^{2}}

\displaystyle=0.576096K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))},

\displaystyle\hat{L}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\hat{\theta}}\frac{\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\omega+(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})))}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}d\omega\sim K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\frac{\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\frac{e^{\bar{c}\hat{\theta}}-1}{\bar{c}}

\displaystyle=0.588497K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))},

\displaystyle\hat{\ell}_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\hat{\theta}+(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})))}-\hat{L}+(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))r(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\sim K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})+\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(\frac{e^{(\bar{c}+\imath)\hat{\theta}}-1}{\bar{c}+\imath}\cdot e^{\imath\theta}-\frac{e^{\bar{c}\hat{\theta}}-1}{\bar{c}})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle=0.306042K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))},

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\hat{r}_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})+\hat{\ell}_{0}\sin(\hat{\theta})-(\hat{Q}_{2}-\hat{Q}_{0})\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\sim 1.15869K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})},

so that the final value is computed via the comparison with the saturated spiral, see Equation (
7.9
), as

\displaystyle r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi})

\displaystyle=(\hat{r}_{2}-\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta}))e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}+\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\bar{\phi})-\hat{\ell}_{0}

\displaystyle\sim\big[(1.15869-e^{\bar{c}\hat{\theta}})e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}+1-0.306042e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}\big]K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}\bar{\phi}}

\displaystyle=0.976359K_{\mathrm{asympt}}\bar{\phi}e^{\bar{c}\bar{\phi}}.

We thus have proved the following result.

Proposition 8.4
.

The optimal candidate solution at
\bar{\phi}
can be estimated as

r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\bar{\phi})=0.976359\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}(\bar{\phi})+\mathcal{O}(1)

for
\bar{\phi}\to\infty
.

In particular, we gain 2.4% using the tent w.r.t. the saturated spiral. However observe that asymptotically
\hat{\ell}_{0}\sim 0.3\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}
, so that the spirals
\tilde{r}_{\mathtt{a}=0}
and
r_{\mathrm{opt}}
are quite different.

9.
Segment-segment case

In the following subsections, we analyze the case where the fastest saturated spiral starts with a segment, and the perturbation of the optimal solution is a tent: we call it
segment-segment case
(or
segment-tent case
). This is the unique possibility for
\bar{\tau}\geq 1
by Proposition
7.7
.

Depending on the relative position of
\phi_{0}
and
\bar{\phi}
,
8
cases have to be considered: we will study them in the next subsections, and we will show that the derivative of the optimal candidate is always positive for all admissible perturbations. The fact that we need to consider several cases is due to the presence of the negativity region: indeed in this case we cannot rely on the estimates of Lemma
7.9
, and the only way is to use the explicit formulas of Lemma
7.4
. For more clarity, see also Figure
49
, where there are the different cases based on the choice of
\bar{\phi}
.
We recall that the angle
\bar{\theta}
is the angle formed by the initial segment
[P_{1},P_{2}]
of the fastest saturated spiral, with
P_{1}=\zeta(s_{0})
.

9.1.
Segment-segment with
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+[\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}),2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\hat{\theta}]
, see Fig.
59

In this case
\hat{r}(\phi;s_{0})=\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})
, because the derivative
\delta\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})\geq 0
: indeed
\bar{\phi}
is outside the region of negativity
N

segm

. This fact gives by Proposition
6.15
the following

Lemma 9.1
.

If
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+[\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}),2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\hat{\theta}]
and
\tilde{r}=\check{r}
is in the segment case, any non-null perturbation of the optimal solution is strictly positive at
\bar{\phi}
.

Proof.

Fix indeed
\bar{\phi}\in[\beta^{-}(\phi_{0}),2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\hat{\theta}]
. In
P_{0}=\zeta(s_{0})
compute the fastest saturated spiral: we know it is optimal whenever
\bar{\theta}-\theta_{0}=\theta>\hat{\theta}
, that is, it is outside the negativity region. Thus in the tent solution
\hat{\ell}_{0}
=0, and this corresponds precisely to the case of Figure
59
.
∎

Figure 59
.
The geometric situation of Section
9.1
: being
\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\phi_{0}-\bar{\theta}>\hat{\theta}
,
\hat{r}=\tilde{r}
and the derivative is
\leq 0
.

9.2.
Segment-segment with
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+(\bar{\theta}-\hat{\theta},\bar{\theta})
, See Fig.
60(a)
.

This situation occurs when
\bar{\phi}
is inside the negativity region
N

segm

: it is thus more convenient to take an initial segment in the direction
\bar{\phi}
, because the derivative
\delta\tilde{r}
is negative according to Proposition
6.7
.

The spiral
\check{\zeta}
, corresponding to
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
, is thus made of a segment
[P_{0}=P_{1}=\check{P}_{0},\check{P}^{-}=\check{P}_{1}]
and a second segment
[\check{P}_{1},\check{P}_{2}]
, forming an angle equal to the critical value
\hat{\theta}
. The assumption to be in the segment-segment case enters here because the second segment corresponds to the fastest saturated spiral starting in
\check{P}_{1}
: in the next Section
10.1.1
we will address the segment-arc case, where after
\check{P}_{1}
the fastest saturated spiral starts with a level-set arc.

In order to prove that any perturbation is positive, we compute the derivative w.r.t. an initial perturbation.

First of all, if
\phi_{0}+\beta^{+}(\phi_{0})+2\pi>\bar{\phi}
, we are not in the optimal solution: indeed this would imply that

\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})-=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})>0.

Hence the situation to be considered is as in Fig.
60(a)
.

(a)

The perturbation of the optimal solution
\check{r}
for the case of Section
9.2
.

(b)

Plot of the function (
9.1
), whose minimal value is
3.45283
.

Figure 60
.
In the first figure, in blue the fastest saturated solution
\tilde{r}
is represented, while
\check{r}
is in red. While
\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})<0
(variation of the distance of the final point from
P_{0}
), it holds
\delta\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})\geq 0
(variation of the distance from
\check{P}^{-}
), giving the optimality in this case. It is also represented the angle
\theta
(magenta), formed by the direction of the perturbation and
\bar{\phi}
.

The variations are

\delta\check{P}_{0}=\delta s_{0}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-\theta)},\quad\delta\check{P}_{1}=\delta\check{P}_{0}+\delta\ell_{0}e^{\imath\bar{\phi}}=\delta s_{0}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-\theta)}+\delta\ell_{0}e^{\imath\bar{\phi}},

\delta\check{P}_{2}=\delta\check{P}_{1}+\delta\ell_{1}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\hat{\theta})}=\delta s_{0}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-\theta)}+\delta\ell_{0}e^{\imath\bar{\phi}}+\delta\ell_{1}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\hat{\theta})},

where we recall that

\ell_{0}=|\check{P}_{1}-\check{P}_{0}|,\quad\ell_{1}=|\check{P}_{2}-\check{P}_{1}|.

The vector relation among the above quantities is

\delta\check{r}_{2}(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\hat{\theta}-\bar{\alpha})}=\delta\check{P}_{2},

because the angle of the optimal ray with
\check{\zeta}
at
\check{P}_{2}
is constantly equal to
\bar{\alpha}
by construction, see Fig.
60(a)
.
Projecting the above equation one obtains

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\frac{\pi}{2}+\hat{\theta}-\bar{\alpha})}\cdot\delta\check{P}_{2}

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)+\delta\ell_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})+\delta\ell_{1}\sin(\bar{\alpha}),

\displaystyle\delta\check{r}_{2}=\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\hat{\theta}-\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)+\delta\ell_{0}\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})+\delta\ell_{1}\cos(\bar{\alpha})

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\frac{\sin(\hat{\theta}+\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}+\delta\ell_{0}\frac{\sin(\hat{\theta})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})},

where we have used the first projection to replace
\delta\ell_{1}
.

The saturation condition at the point
\check{P}_{2}+\delta\check{P}_{2}
gives

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\delta\check{r}_{2}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\big(\delta s_{0}+\delta\ell_{0}+\delta\ell_{1}\big)

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\big(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\big)+\delta\ell_{0}(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})),

so that

\delta\ell_{0}=-\delta s_{0}\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})},

\displaystyle\delta\check{r}_{2}

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\frac{\sin(\hat{\theta}+\theta)(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))-\sin(\hat{\theta})(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})(\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}))}

\displaystyle=\delta s_{0}\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\big(\sin(\theta)(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))+(1-\cos(\theta))\sin(\hat{\theta})\big).

As in Corollary
6.6
, the ODE for the perturbation divided by
\delta s_{0}
becomes

\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi}-\theta-)=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)}\delta\check{r}_{2},

\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi}-)=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}\delta\check{r}_{2}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta},

and then the final value is computed as

\displaystyle\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})

\displaystyle=\delta\check{r}_{2}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}-\delta\ell_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\big(\sin(\theta)(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))+(1-\cos(\theta))\sin(\hat{\theta})\big)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}-\theta)-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta}+\theta)}{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta},

where we have used the definition of
\hat{\theta}
, namely (see (
6.7
))

\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}=1.

A numerical plot of

(9.1)

\theta\mapsto\frac{\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta}=\frac{1}{\theta}\bigg(\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta}+\theta)}{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}\bigg)

is in Fig.
60(b)
: the function is strictly positive, with minimal value

\lim_{\theta\to 0}\frac{1}{\theta}\bigg(\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta}+\theta)}{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}\bigg)=\frac{\sin(\hat{\theta})}{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}-\cot(\bar{\alpha})=3.45283.

We have proved the following result.

Lemma 9.2
.

If
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+(\bar{\theta}-\hat{\theta},\bar{\theta})
, any non-null perturbation of the optimal solution is strictly positive at
\bar{\phi}
for
\theta\in[0,\pi]
.

The geometric situation of Section

.

(b)

The geometric situation of Section
9.4
.

(c)

The geometric situation of Section
9.5
.

(d)

9.6
.

(e)

The geometric situation of Section

.

(f)

The geometric situation of Section

.

Remark 9.3
.

From now on we will write the formulas for the perturbation of
\tilde{\zeta},\check{\zeta}
omitting the term
\delta s_{0}
.

9.3.
Segment-segment with
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\bar{\theta},2\pi+\bar{\alpha}+\bar{\theta}-\theta-\hat{\theta})
: see Fig.
61(a)

In this case the full tent is constructed, so that we can use the formulas of Lemma
7.4
to compute the perturbation
\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})
. In this case, we have that the base of the tent is not perturbed, so that we are in the situation of Fig.
53
with

\hat{\zeta}=\{(0,0)\}\quad\text{because}\quad\check{\phi}_{0}=\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}\geq\bar{\phi}_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha},\ \check{\phi}_{1}=\check{\phi}_{0}+\hat{\theta}\leq\phi_{0}+2\pi-\bar{\theta}-\theta.

The computations are thus explicit: the quantities of Lemma
7.4
are computed in this case as

\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{0};s_{0}),\quad\hat{\ell}_{0}=\frac{\hat{r}_{0}(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})},

\hat{r}_{2}=\frac{\hat{r}_{0}\sin(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})+\hat{\ell}_{0}\sin(\hat{\theta})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})},\quad\hat{\ell}_{1}=\frac{\hat{r}_{2}-\hat{r}_{1}}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}-\hat{\ell}_{0}.

The numerical plot of

\displaystyle\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}}{\theta^{2}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}}{\theta^{2}}\bigg[(\hat{r}_{2}-\delta\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{2};s_{0}))e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}+\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})-\hat{\ell}_{0}\bigg]

is in Fig.
62(a)
: its minimal value is
>0.2
.

We have proved the following result.

Lemma 9.4
.

If
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\bar{\theta},2\pi+\bar{\alpha}+\bar{\theta}-\hat{\theta})
, any non-null perturbation of the optimal solution is strictly positive at
\bar{\phi}
for
\theta\in[0,\pi]
.

(a)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}\bar{\phi}}\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta^{2}}
.

(b)

Minimal value of the function

, i.e. when

.

9.4.
Segment-segment with
\check{\phi}_{0}\leq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta,\check{\phi}_{2}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\bar{\theta}-\theta,\bar{\theta})
: see Fig.
61(b)

Since the angle
\bar{\phi}
is fixed, the idea here is that we perturb the point
\hat{Q}_{2}
, while the perturbation of the point
\hat{Q}_{0}
is
\delta\hat{Q}_{0}=0
. Because of the relative position of
\bar{\phi}
with respect to the perturbed point
\hat{Q}_{2}+\delta\hat{Q}_{2}
, we see that the perturbation of the point
\hat{Q}_{2}
is the unit vector
e^{i(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta)}
, where
\theta=\bar{\theta}-\theta_{0}
is the angle introduced in Section
6
.
Hence the formulas of Lemma
7.4
become

\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}=e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta)}=e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}-\theta+2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})-\bar{\phi})}=e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}))}e^{\imath((2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(2-\bar{\tau})-\theta)},

\big(\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\big)\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}=\cos\big((2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(2-\bar{\tau})-\theta-\hat{\theta}\big),

\big(\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\big)\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}e^{\imath\left(\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}\right)}=\cos\bigg((2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(2-\bar{\tau})-\theta-\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta}+\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg),

\delta\hat{L}=1,\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{0})=\delta\rho(\check{\tau}_{0})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\check{\tau}_{0}}.

In terms of
\check{\tau}_{0}
the interval of interest is

1-\frac{\theta+\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\leq\check{\tau}_{0}<1-\frac{\max\{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}.

The functions
\hat{\ell}_{0},\hat{r}_{2},\hat{\ell}_{1}
are computed according to Lemma
7.4
.

In Fig.
63(a)
we numerically plot the function

(9.2)

\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}=\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}}{\theta^{2}}\big[e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}(\delta\hat{r}(\check{\phi}_{2})-\delta\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{2}))+\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\delta\hat{\ell}_{0}\big],

corresponding to the quantity computed in Equation (
7.9
), which follows with the comparison with the saturated spiral. We compute this quantity as a function of
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{taubar}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bar{\tau}}}=(1-\varsigma)(1-\frac{\theta+\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})+\varsigma(2-\frac{\max\{{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})
,
\varsigma\in[0,1]
. The function is strictly positive, with minimal value greater than
0.2
in
\theta=\pi,\bar{\tau}=2-\frac{\pi+\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}
.

9.2
).

) with

We thus have proved the following lemma.

Lemma 9.5
.

If
\check{\phi}_{0}\leq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta,\check{\phi}_{2}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\bar{\theta}-\theta,\bar{\theta})
, then
\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq 0
for
\theta\in[0,\pi]
.

Remark 9.6
.

Note that the asymptotic behavior
\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})\simeq\theta
as
\theta\to 0
for these angles (see Fig.
31
) becomes now
\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})\simeq\theta^{2}
, showing that the tent has a regularizing effect on the dependence w.r.t.
\theta
, while
\delta\tilde{r}\sim-1
.

9.5.
Segment-segment with
\check{\phi}_{0}\leq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta,\check{\phi}_{1}\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}]
: see Fig.

Because of the conditions on
\bar{\phi}
, we have in this case that the point
\hat{Q}_{2}
and its perturbation lie in the saturated part, so that

\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau}))}=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1))}e^{\imath(\hat{\theta}-\bar{\alpha})},

\big(\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\big)\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}\cos(\bar{\alpha}),

\big(\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}\big)\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}e^{\imath\left(-\frac{\pi}{2}+\bar{\alpha}\right)}=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}\cos\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2}-2\bar{\alpha}\bigg),

See the auxiliary picture
64
for reference. In particular we have also

\displaystyle\delta\hat{L}

\displaystyle\big[\text{Equation}\ (\ref{Equa:delta_P_2P_1_segm})\big]\quad
