9.7.
Segment-segment with
\check{\phi}_{0}\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta,\check{\phi}_{1}\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}
: see Fig.
61(e)

In this case we have

\displaystyle\Delta\hat{Q}=\delta\hat{Q}_{2}-\delta\hat{Q}_{0}

\displaystyle=\delta\tilde{r}(\check{\phi}_{2}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha})e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau}))}-e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\theta-(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1)))}

\displaystyle=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1+\hat{\tau}))}e^{\imath(\hat{\theta}-\bar{\alpha})}

\displaystyle\quad-e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1))}e^{\imath(-\theta-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1)))},

We remark that the change of variables we have used is

\phi=\phi_{0}+\bar{\theta}-\bar{\alpha}+(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\tau,\quad\hat{\tau}=\frac{\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}.

In particular we have denoted by
\bar{\tau}
that value corresponding to
\bar{\phi}
.

\Delta\hat{Q}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta}))}=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(-\theta-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2)-\hat{\theta}),

\Delta\hat{Q}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}))}=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}\cos\bigg(\frac{\pi}{2}-2\bar{\alpha}\bigg)-\cos\bigg(-\theta-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2)-\bigg(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg)\bigg),

\displaystyle\delta\hat{L}

\displaystyle=\delta|P_{2}-P_{1}|-\frac{r(\phi_{2})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\phi_{2}+\int_{0}^{(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}d\phi

\displaystyle\big[(\ref{Equa:delta_P_2P_1_segm})\big]\quad

\displaystyle=-1+\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}+\int_{0}^{(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})}\frac{\delta\tilde{r}(\phi)}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}d\phi

\displaystyle=-1+\frac{1-\cos(\theta)}{\sin^{2}(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2+\hat{\tau})},

\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-1)e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1)}.

The interval of interest is

2-\frac{\min\{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\leq\bar{\tau}<2,\quad\theta\in(0,\pi].

In Fig.
67(a)
we numerically plot the function
\frac{\delta\check{r}(\bar{\tau})e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}}{\theta^{2}}
as a function of
\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)(1-\frac{\min\{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})+2\varsigma
,
\varsigma\in(0,1)
: its minimal value is for
\varsigma=0,\theta=\pi/2
and
>0.59
(Fig.
67(b)
).

(a)

Plot of the function
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})/\theta^{2}
.

(b)

Plot of the function
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})/\theta^{2}
for
\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Figure 67
.
Analysis of Section
9.7
.

We thus have proved the following lemma.

Lemma 9.9
.

If
\check{\phi}_{0}\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}-\theta,\check{\phi}_{2}\geq\phi_{0}+2\pi+\bar{\theta}
, then
\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq 0.59\theta^{2}
.

9.8.
Segment-segment with
\tau-2\geq 0
: see Fig.
61(f)

In this region the base of the tent is a convex curve, because
\delta\tilde{r}\geq 0
: we can thus apply Lemma
7.9
, we need just the positivity of

(9.4)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}\bar{\phi}}}{\theta^{2}}\bigg(\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\tilde{r}(\hat{\phi}_{0})\bigg)=\frac{1}{\theta^{2}}\bigg(\delta\rho(\bar{\tau})-\frac{(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})2}}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-2)\bigg),\quad\bar{\tau}>2.

A numerical plot is in Fig.
68
, where one observe that is is positive and strictly increasing.

(a)

Plot of the function (
9.4
).

(b)

Plot of the function (
9.4
) for
\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Figure 68
.
Analysis of Section
9.8
.

We thus conclude that

Lemma 9.10
.

If
\bar{\tau}\geq 2
, then
\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq 0
.

10.
Study of the perturbation to the optimal solution in the presence of an arc

In this section we address the case when the optimal candidate we are going to perturb contains a level set arc. More precisely, four situations can occur:

(1)

the optimal candidate spiral
\check{r}
starts with a segment at
\phi_{0}
in the direction
\bar{\phi}
, and exit the negativity region in the arc case, Section
10.1.1
and Fig.
69(a)
;

(2)

the optimal candidate spiral
\check{r}
starts with an arc, then a segment at
\phi=\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}
in the direction
\bar{\phi}
, and exits the negativity region in the arc case, Section
10.1.2
and Fig.
70
;

(3)

the optimal candidate spiral
\check{r}
starts with an arc, then a segment at
\phi=\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}
in the direction
\bar{\phi}
, and exits the negativity region in the segment case, Section
10.2.1
and Fi.
72(a)
;

(4)

the optimal candidate spiral
\check{r}
starts with an arc, then a segment and a saturated spiral, next at
\phi=\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2}
we have a tent, Section
10.2.2
: the 10 different respective positions of the arc and the tent are presented in Fig.
73
.

By Proposition
7.7
, only the last case can occur for
\bar{\tau}\geq 1
. In particular, when we are in the saturated region for
\hat{\phi}_{0}
(the angle at which the tent starts), the tent is admissible, so that the following situation is not happening: the optimal candidate starts with an arc, then a segment and a saturated spiral, next at
\phi=\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\bar{\alpha}
there is a segment in the direction
\bar{\phi}
and the last round starts with an arc.

10.1.
Segment-arc or arc-arc cases

These situations can happen only when
\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\phi_{0}\leq\omega
, i.e.
\bar{\tau}<1
by Proposition
7.7
.

10.1.1.
Segment-arc with
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\bar{\theta}-\theta,\bar{\theta})
: see Fig.
69(a)

(a)

The geometric situation of Section
10.1.1
.

(b)

The geometry to compute the variations of the points in the arc case.

Figure 69
.
Analysis of Section
10.1.1
.

Setting for simplicity
\bar{\phi}=0
and with
\theta
the angle of the perturbation of the point
P_{0}
, the variations of the positions of the points is (see Fig.
69(b)
):

\delta P_{0}=e^{-\imath\theta},\quad\delta\check{P}^{-}=\delta P_{0}+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}e^{\imath\bar{\phi}}=\delta P_{0}+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}e^{\imath 0},\quad\delta\check{\phi}^{-}=\frac{\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath\check{\theta}}}{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})},

\delta\check{P}_{1}=\big[\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\big]e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}-\frac{\pi}{2})}+\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{1})\delta\check{\phi}_{1}e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi})},\quad\delta\check{\phi}_{1}=\frac{\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath\check{\theta}}}{\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})}+\delta\Delta\check{\phi},

where the first term is the variation of the level set and the second is the variation of the angle along the level set. Using Formula (
6.16
) we have used the fact that
\check{\phi}^{-}=-\frac{\pi}{2}+\check{\theta}-2\pi
. Moreover we have

\delta\check{P}_{2}=\delta\check{P}_{1}+\delta\check{\ell}_{1}e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi})}+\delta\check{\phi}_{1}\check{\ell}_{1}e^{i(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}+\frac{\pi}{2})}.

For reference compare the previous formulas with (
6.16
),(
6.17
).
The optimality condition requires by Lemma
6.16
that

\check{\theta}=h_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi}),\quad\delta\check{\theta}=\delta\check{\phi}^{-}=\frac{dh_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi})}{d\Delta\check{\phi}}\delta\Delta\check{\phi},

and the saturation condition reads as

\begin{split}0&=\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}-\bar{\alpha})}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(1+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})\delta\check{\phi}^{-}+\Delta\check{\phi}\big(\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\big)+\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{1})\delta\check{\phi}_{1}+\delta\check{\ell}_{1}\bigg)\\
&=\big(\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi})}\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\sin(\bar{\alpha})\big)\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(1+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})\delta\check{\phi}^{-}+\Delta\check{\phi}\big(\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\big)+\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{1})\delta\check{\phi}_{1}+\delta\check{\ell}_{1}\bigg)\\
&=\big((\hat{r}_{1}(\hat{\phi}_{1})\delta\phi_{1}+\delta\hat{\ell}_{1})\cos(\bar{\alpha})+\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\sin(\bar{\alpha})\big)\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(1+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})\delta\check{\phi}^{-}+\Delta\check{\phi}\big(\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\big)+\check{r}(\check{\phi}_{1})\delta\check{\phi}_{1}+\delta\check{\ell}_{1}\bigg)\\
&=\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\bigg(1+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\check{r}(\check{\phi}^{-})\delta\check{\phi}^{-}+\Delta\check{\phi}\big(\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\big)\bigg).\end{split}

The final value is computed by

\begin{split}\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})&=\frac{\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\theta}-\Delta\check{\phi})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}-\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\\
&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\bigg(1+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\big(\cos(\theta+\check{\theta})+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\cos(\check{\theta})\big)+\Delta\check{\phi}\big(\sin(\theta+\check{\theta})+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\sin(\check{\theta})\big)\bigg)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\theta}-\Delta\check{\phi})}\\
&-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}-\delta\check{\ell}_{0}.\end{split}

Using (
6.22
) we simplify into

\begin{split}\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\big(1-\cos(\theta+\check{\theta})+\Delta\check{\phi}\sin(\theta+\check{\theta})\big)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\theta}-\Delta\check{\phi})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}\\
&=e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}\bigg(\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}\big(1-\cos(\theta+\check{\theta})+\Delta\check{\phi}\sin(\theta+\check{\theta})\big)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-(\check{\theta}+\theta)-\Delta\check{\phi})}-1\bigg),\end{split}

which is
\geq 0
unless
\theta=0
by Lemma
6.16
.

We thus have the following result.

Lemma 10.1
.

If
\bar{\phi}\in\phi_{0}+2\pi+[\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta,\frac{\pi}{2})
, then
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq 0
.

10.1.2.
Arc-arc with
2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}\leq\bar{\phi}-\phi_{0}\leq 2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi
: see Fig.
70

Figure 70
.
The geometric situation of Section
10.1.2
.

The angle
\Delta\phi
is the angle corresponding to the arc of the level set for the fastest saturated spiral
\tilde{\zeta}
. We first observe that the angle at the point
\check{P}_{2}
is

(10.1)

\begin{split}\check{\phi}_{2}-\phi_{0}&\leq\tan(\bar{\alpha})+\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}\\
&\leq\tan(\bar{\alpha})+\max\big\{\Delta\check{\phi}+h_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi})\big\}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\bar{\alpha}<5.4<2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta,\end{split}

so that the end point is always inside the first round. We thus have

\delta P_{0}=e^{\imath(\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta)},\quad\delta\check{P}_{0}=\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2})},

\delta\check{P}^{-}=\delta\check{\ell}_{0}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi)}+\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\frac{\pi}{2})},\quad\delta\check{P}_{1}=\check{r}_{1}\delta\check{\omega}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\omega})}+\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\omega}-\frac{\pi}{2})},

\delta\check{P}_{2}=\delta\check{P}_{1}+\delta\check{\ell}_{1}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\check{\omega})}-\check{\ell}_{1}\delta\check{\omega}e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}+\check{\omega}-\frac{\pi}{2})}.

Using
\Delta\phi=\bar{\phi}-2\pi-\phi_{0}-\frac{\pi}{2}
and denoting

\check{\omega}=\check{\theta}+\Delta\check{\phi}=h_{1}(\Delta\check{\phi})+\Delta\check{\phi},

the saturation condition is

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\omega}-\bar{\alpha})}-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\Big((1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\check{r}^{-}\delta\check{\phi}^{-}+\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\Delta\check{\phi}+\check{r}_{1}\delta\check{\phi}_{1}+\delta\check{\ell}_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta\check{P}_{1}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\omega}-\bar{\alpha})}-\check{\ell}_{1}\delta\check{\omega}\sin(\bar{\alpha})

\displaystyle\quad-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\Big((1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi)+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{i(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta})}+\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\Delta\check{\phi}+\delta\check{P}_{1}\cdot e^{i(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\omega})}\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\sin(\bar{\alpha})-\check{\ell}_{1}\delta\check{\omega}\sin(\bar{\alpha})

\displaystyle\quad-\cos(\bar{\alpha})\Big(\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi\big)+\sin(\theta)\sin(\check{\theta})+\sin(\theta)\cos(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\big(1-\cos(\check{\theta})+\sin(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}\big)\Big).

The final value is

(10.2)

\begin{split}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})&=\frac{\delta\check{P}_{2}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\omega}-\frac{\pi}{2})}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\omega})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-\sin(\theta)\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}-\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\\
&=\frac{\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}-\check{\ell}_{1}\delta\check{\omega}}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\omega})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-\sin(\theta)\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}-\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\\
&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\omega})}\Big(\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi\big)+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}-\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{i(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta})}+\delta\check{P}^{-}\cdot e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2\pi+\check{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}\Delta\check{\phi}\Big)\\
&\quad-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-\sin(\theta)\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}-\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\\
&=\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\omega})}\Big(\big(1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi\big)\\
&\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sin(\theta)\sin(\check{\theta})+\sin(\theta)\cos(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}+\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\big(1-\cos(\check{\theta})+\sin(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}\big)\Big)\\
&\quad-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}+\cos(\theta)e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-\sin(\theta)\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}-\delta\check{\ell}_{0}\\
&=\frac{1-\cos(\theta+\check{\theta})+\sin(\theta+\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{1-\cos(\check{\theta})+\sin(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}+\cos(\theta)(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1)-\sin(\theta)\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}\\
&=\frac{1-\cos(\theta+\check{\theta})+\sin(\theta+\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{1-\cos(\check{\theta})+\sin(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi}}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}+\cos(\theta+\bar{\alpha})\frac{e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\Delta\phi}-1}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})},\end{split}

where we have used (
6.22
), and we rely on the observation that the final angle is before
2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta
. The second derivative w.r.t.
\Delta\phi
is

\partial^{2}_{\Delta\phi}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})=-\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\Delta\phi+\theta)}\big(\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}+\theta)e^{-\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}\big)\leq 0,

so that the minimal values are for
\Delta\phi=0,\tan(\bar{\alpha})
. The first case corresponds to the previous section, while for
\Delta\phi=\tan(\bar{\alpha})
we plot the function
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})/\theta
in Fig.
71(b)
in a neighborhood of the curve determined by (
6.22
): the function is positive
>4.5\theta
.

(a)

A small neighborhood of the region
\frac{\cot(\bar{\alpha})}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})}(1-\cos(\check{\theta})+\sin(\check{\theta})\Delta\check{\phi})e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\check{\theta}-\Delta\check{\phi})}=1
: in orange the plane
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times\{0\}
and in blue the previous function.

(b)

The function
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})/\theta
for various values of
\theta\in(0,\pi/2]
in the same neighborhood.

Figure 71
.
Analysis of the final value of
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})
, Section
10.1.2
.

Lemma 10.2
.

In this geometric setting it holds
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq 4.5\theta
.

10.2.
Arc-segment case

The situation is when the initial spiral
\tilde{r}
starts with an arc, and later
\check{r}
closes with a tent. This is the unique situation in the saturated region. There are several cases depending on the position of the segment w.r.t. the arc.

10.2.1.
The tent starts inside the arc: Fig.
72(a)

(a)

The geometric situation of Section
10.2.1
.

(b)

10.3
) divided by
\theta
.

.

.

In this case the analysis is the same as in the arc-arc case, by setting
\Delta\check{\phi}=0
and
\check{\theta}=\hat{\theta}
: as before we are left with the case
\Delta\phi=\tan(\bar{\alpha})
, thus the final value is

(10.3)

\displaystyle\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\cos(\theta+\hat{\theta})+\sin(\theta)\tan(\bar{\alpha})}{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}-e^{1+\cot(\bar{\alpha})\theta}+\frac{\cos(\theta+\bar{\alpha})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}(e-1).

The plot of this function is in Fig.
72(b)
.

Lemma 10.3
.

In the above situation the perturbation is positive
>14\theta
.

10.2.2.
Arc-tent case with the final tent in the saturated region

The 10 cases representing the relative positions of the initial arc and the final tent are represented in Fig.
71
.

Figure 73
.
The 10 possible relative positions of the arc and the tent in the saturated region: in green the number of the case considered in Section
10.2.2
.

We will use the tent formulas of Section
7.2
.

Case 1:
\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi-\bar{\alpha})-\phi_{0}\in(\omega-\bar{\alpha},2\pi+\frac{\pi}{2}-\theta-\hat{\theta})
:

in this case
\delta\hat{Q}_{0}=0
,
\delta\hat{Q}_{2}=0
,
\delta\hat{L}=0
, so that (see formulas in Lemma
7.4
)

\hat{\ell}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}_{0}\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})},

(10.4)

\delta\hat{r}_{2}-\delta\tilde{r}_{2}=\delta\tilde{r}_{0}\bigg(\frac{\cos(\bar{\alpha})-\cos(\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}-e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})\hat{\theta})}\bigg)>0.00669\delta\tilde{r}_{0}>0,

and we thus just estimate

\displaystyle\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})

\displaystyle=\big(\delta\hat{r}_{2}-\delta\tilde{r}_{2})e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(2\pi+\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})}+\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\hat{\ell}_{0}

\displaystyle>\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\delta\tilde{r}_{0}\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}.

A numerical evaluation of the above function is in Fig.
74
as a function of
\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)+\varsigma(2-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi+\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})
,
\varsigma\in(0,1)
, where one sees that it is larger than
.35e^{\bar{c}\phi}\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)
.

Figure 74
.
Numerical plot of the function
e^{-\bar{c}\phi}(\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi})-\delta\tilde{r}_{0}\frac{1-\cos(\hat{\theta})}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})})
and its worst case for
\theta=\frac{\pi}{2},\Delta\phi=0
, Case 1 of Section
10.2.2
.

Case 2:
\bar{\tau}\in(1,1+\tau_{1}),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}-1\in(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
:

here the end point of the tent is in the region
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=(1-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}},1-\frac{\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})
, while the initial point
\check{P}_{0}
is in the saturated arc before, so that using
\tau=0
as the reference direction for computing the angles (i.e.
\phi_{0}+\frac{\pi}{2}+\Delta\phi-\bar{\alpha}
is mapped to
0
), we have:

\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta,\quad\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi,

\delta\Delta Q=e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)},\quad\delta\Delta Q\cdot e^{\imath(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1+\hat{\tau})}=\cos\big((2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2)+\Delta\phi+\theta+\hat{\theta}\big),

\delta\Delta Q\cdot e^{\imath(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-1+\hat{\tau})+\bar{\alpha}-\frac{\pi}{2}}=\cos\bigg((2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\tau}-2)+\Delta\phi+\theta+\hat{\theta}+\bar{\alpha}-\frac{\pi}{2}\bigg),

\delta\hat{L}=1,\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

Recall that

\hat{\tau}=\frac{\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}.

The plot of the function

(10.5)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)},\quad\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)\bigg(2-\frac{\theta+\hat{\theta}+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg)+\varsigma\bigg(2-\frac{\max\{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg),\ \varsigma\in(0,1),

computed according to the formulas of Lemma
7.4
, is in Fig.
75
, which is strictly positive:

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\hat{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\geq 0.54.

Figure 75
.
Final value for Case 2 and of its minimum, (
10.5
): the orange surface corresponds to
\Delta\phi=0
and the minimum to
\Delta\phi=0,\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Case 3:
\bar{\tau}\in(1,1+\tau_{1}),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}-1\in(\tau_{2},1)
:

here the initial point of the tent is before
\tau_{1}=1-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}
, and the final point is in
(\tau_{2},1)=(1-\frac{\theta}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}},1)
. As before setting
\tau=0
as reference direction, we have thus

\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta,\quad\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}<\bar{\alpha},\quad\theta<\hat{\theta},

\delta\Delta Q=\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})},\quad\delta\hat{L}=1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\bigg(\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}+\Delta\phi-\bar{\alpha}\bigg),\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

The plot of the function

(10.6)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)},\quad\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)\bigg(2-\frac{\hat{\theta}+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg)+\varsigma\bigg(2-\frac{\max\{\theta+\Delta\phi,\hat{\theta}\}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg),\ \varsigma\in(0,1),

is in Fig.
76
, showing that

e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})>0.74\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi).

Figure 76
.
Final value for Case 3, Equation (
10.6
): the orange surface corresponds to
\Delta\phi=0
, and the minimal case is for
\Delta\phi=0,\theta=\hat{\theta}
(in the numerical plot we consider
\theta=\hat{\theta}-10^{-4}
to avoid numerical artifacts).

Case 4:
\bar{\tau}\in(1,1+\tau_{1}),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}>2
:

here the initial value is before
\tau_{1}=1-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}
region and the final value is after
\tau=1
:

\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta,\quad\bar{\phi}>2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})-\hat{\theta},\quad\Delta\phi+\theta<\hat{\theta}.

In particular
\delta\hat{Q}_{0}=0
,
\delta\Delta Q=\delta\hat{Q}_{2}
which reads as

\delta\Delta Q=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})},

\delta\hat{L}=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})},

\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

The plot of the function

(10.7)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)},\quad\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)\bigg(2-\frac{\hat{\theta}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg)+\varsigma\bigg(2-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg),\ \varsigma\in(0,1),

is in Fig.
77
, which is strictly positive
>0.74
.

Figure 77
.
Final value for Case 4, Equation (
10.7
): the minimal values occur for
\Delta\phi=0,\theta=0
.

Case 5:
\bar{\tau}\in(1+\tau_{1},1+\tau_{2}),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}\in(1+\tau_{1},1+\tau_{2})
:

here the tent is inside
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=(1-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}},1-\frac{\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})
:

\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi,\quad\theta>\hat{\theta},

\delta\Delta Q=0,\quad\delta\hat{L}=0,\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

The above condition implies that
\theta\geq\hat{\theta}
. The plot of the function

(10.8)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)},\quad\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)\bigg(2-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg)+\varsigma\bigg(2-\frac{\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg),\ \varsigma\in(0,1),

is in Fig.
78
, which is strictly positive
>0.55
.

Figure 78
.

10.8
): the minimal values are obtained for

.

Case 6:
\bar{\tau}\in(1+\tau_{1},1+\tau_{2}),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}\in(1+\tau_{2},1)
:

here the tent starts inside
(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})=(1-\frac{\theta+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}},1-\frac{\theta}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}})
and ends in
(\tau_{2},1)
:

\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi,\quad\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}<\bar{\alpha},\quad\Delta\phi+\theta>\hat{\theta},

\delta\Delta Q=\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}-e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)},\quad\delta\hat{L}=-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta}+\Delta\phi),\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

The above condition implies that
\theta\geq\hat{\theta}
. The plot of the function

(10.9)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta},\quad\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)\bigg(2-\frac{\min\{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg)+\varsigma\bigg(2-\frac{\max\{\Delta\phi,\hat{\theta}\}}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg),\ \varsigma\in(0,1),

is in Fig.
79
, which is strictly positive
>.38\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)
.

Figure 79
.

Case 7:
\bar{\tau}\in(1+\tau_{1},1+\tau_{2}),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}>2

here the tent starts inside (

and ends after

\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi,\quad\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta},\quad\Delta\phi<\hat{\theta},

\delta\Delta Q=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}-e^{\imath(\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi-\theta)},

\delta\hat{L}=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}-1,

\bar{\tau}>1+\tau_{2},\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}<2

here the tent is inside
(\tau_{2},1)

\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}<\bar{\alpha},\quad\Delta\phi>\hat{\theta},

\delta\Delta Q=\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta}-\frac{\pi}{2})}-\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}-\frac{\pi}{2})},\quad\delta\hat{L}=\sin(\theta)\hat{\theta},\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

Case 9:
\bar{\tau}\in(1+\tau_{2},2),\bar{\tau}+\hat{\tau}>2
:

here the tent starts inside
(\tau_{2},1)
and ends after
\tau=1
:

\bar{\alpha}-\Delta\phi<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\alpha},\quad\bar{\alpha}<\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}+\hat{\theta},

\delta\Delta Q=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}-\sin(\theta)e^{\imath(\bar{\phi}-4\pi-\bar{\alpha}-\frac{\pi}{2})},

\delta\hat{L}=\frac{1-\cos(\theta)+\sin(\theta)\Delta\phi}{\sin(\bar{\alpha})^{2}}(e^{\cot(\bar{\alpha})(\bar{\phi}-2(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})+\hat{\theta})}-1)+\sin(\theta)(-\bar{\phi}+4\pi+\bar{\alpha})),\quad\delta\hat{r}_{0}=\delta\tilde{r}(\bar{\phi}-(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})).

The above condition implies that
\theta\geq\hat{\theta}
. The plot of the function

(10.12)

\frac{e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})\bar{\tau}}\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)},\quad\bar{\tau}=(1-\varsigma)\bigg(2-\frac{\theta+\hat{\theta}+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg)+\varsigma\bigg(2-\frac{\max\{\theta,\hat{\theta}\}+\Delta\phi}{2\pi+\bar{\alpha}}\bigg),\ \varsigma\in(0,1),

is in Fig.

, which is strictly positive
>0.62
.

.

Case 10:
\bar{\tau}>2
:

here the base of the tent is after
\tau=0
, and in particular the perturbed spiral
\delta\tilde{r}
is positive. Similarly to Section
9.3
, we can thus use Lemma
7.9
and just study the function

(10.13)

\bar{\tau}\mapsto\frac{1}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\bigg(\delta\rho(\bar{\tau})-\frac{(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-1)\bigg),

with
\rho(\tau)
given by Corollary
6.14
. The numerical plot of the above function for
\tau\in(2,5)
is in Fig.
83
and of its
\tau
-derivative for
\tau\in(4,5)
is in Fig.
84
: we can thus apply Lemma
3.14
and deduce that

\frac{1}{\theta(\theta+\Delta\phi)}\bigg(\delta\rho(\bar{\tau})-\frac{(1-\cos(\hat{\theta}))e^{-\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})}}{\cos(\bar{\alpha}-\hat{\theta})-\cos(\bar{\alpha})}\delta\rho(\bar{\tau}-1)\bigg)>0.59.

Figure 83
.
Final value for Case 10, Equation (
10.13
): the minimal values are for
\Delta\phi=0,\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
.

Figure 84
.
Derivative for Case 10, Equation (
10.13
): the minimal values are for
\Delta\phi=0,\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
.

We collect all the above statements into the following

Lemma 10.4
.

If the final tent is in the saturated region of
\check{r}
, then
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})\geq 0
, and it is
0
only if the angle
\theta=0
.

This lemma, together with Lemmas
10.1
,
10.2
,
10.3
, shows that
\delta\check{r}(\bar{\phi})>0
, and it is
0
if and only if
\theta=0
, concluding the proof of Theorem
1.12
using also the results of Section
9
.

1/Sin[baralpha] - 1 - Tan[baralpha] - Cot[baralpha]

Section
6

Arc (segment) coefficients: these are the coefficients for the general perturbation, Corollary
6.14
. The coefficients for the segment case are obtained from these by setting
\Delta\phi=0
as in Remark
6.12
and Corollary
6.6

S0arc[theta_, Deltaphi_] :=
Cot[baralpha] (1 - Cos[theta] + Sin[theta] Deltaphi)/Sin[baralpha]

S1arc[theta_,
Deltaphi_] := -Sin[baralpha] Exp[
barc (theta + Deltaphi)]/(2 Pi + baralpha)

S2arc[theta_, Deltaphi_] :=
Sin[baralpha] Cos[theta] Exp[barc Deltaphi]/(2 Pi + baralpha)

S3arc[theta_] := - Sin[theta]

S4arc[theta_, Deltaphi_] :=
Cos[baralpha] (-2 Cot[
baralpha] (1 - Cos[theta] + Sin[theta] Deltaphi) +
Sin[theta])/(2 Pi + baralpha)

Darc[theta_, Deltaphi_] :=
Sin[baralpha] (-Cot[baralpha] (1 - Cos[theta] +
Sin[theta] Deltaphi) + Sin[theta])/(2 Pi + baralpha)

S5arc[theta_,
Deltaphi_] := -Sin[
baralpha] (-Cot[baralpha] (1 - Cos[theta] + Sin[theta] Deltaphi) +
Sin[theta])/(2 Pi + baralpha)^2

tau1arc[theta_, Deltaphi_] := 1 - (theta + Deltaphi)/(2 Pi + baralpha)

tau2arc[Deltaphi_] := 1 - Deltaphi/(2 Pi + baralpha)

This is the generic form of the solution, without the delta Dirac delta
\mathfrak{d}_{\tau=1}
, Corollaries
6.6
and
6.14

rhogeneric[tau_, theta_, Deltaphi_] :=
S0arc[theta, Deltaphi] g[tau] +
S1arc[theta, Deltaphi] g[tau - tau1arc[theta, Deltaphi]] +
S2arc[theta, Deltaphi] g[tau - tau2arc[Deltaphi]] +
S3arc[theta] m[tau, tau2arc[Deltaphi], 1] +
S4arc[theta, Deltaphi] g[tau - 1] +
S5arc[theta, Deltaphi] g[tau - 2]

Glossary

$\angle(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})$

angle between the vectors $\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}$

$\bar{\alpha}$

critical angle, corresponding to the critical speed $\bar{\sigma}=\frac{1}{\cos(\bar{\alpha})}$

$\bar{\phi}$

last rotation angle, Theorem \ref{Cor:angle_preprest}

$\bar{\sigma}$

critical speed, Corollary \ref{Cor:eigen_angle}

$\bar{\theta}$

angle of the initial segment $[P_{1},P_{2}]$ for the family $\tilde{\zeta}(\cdot;s_{0})$

$\bar{\gamma}_{x}$

optimal ray for the point $x$

$\bar{\Gamma}$

set of optimal rays

$\beta(\phi)$

angle between the optimal ray and the tangent to the spiral at its end point

$\mathbb{C}$

complex numbers

$\check{\phi}_{0}$

angle of the point $\check{P}_{0}$

$\check{P}_{0}$

initial point of the segment of length $\ell_{0}$ in the direction $\bar{\phi}$

$\check{P}_{1}$

initial point of the last segment of the fastest saturated spiral

$\check{r}(\phi;s_{0})$

optimal spiral at the angle $\bar{\phi}$

$\Delta\check{\phi}$

angle of the level set for the last round of the optimal solution $\check{r}(\bar{\phi};s_{0})$

$\Delta\phi$

opening angle for the arc on the level set

$\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}$

derivative of the Dirac delta $\mathfrak{d}$

$\mathfrak{d}_{0}$

Dirac's delta measure in $0$

$\mathfrak{d}_{s}$

Dirac's delta measure centered in $s$

$\ell_{0}$

length of the segment $[\check{P}_{0},P^{-}]$ of direction $\bar{\phi}$

$\ell_{1}$

length of the last segment $[\check{P}_{1},\check{P}_{2}]$ of the fastest saturated spiral

$\Gamma$

set of admissible curves for the fire spreading

$\hat{\ell}_{0}$

length of the first segment in the tent or arc computations

$\hat{\ell}_{1}$

length of the second segment in the tent or arc computations

$\hat{\tau}$

angle shift \lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{thetahat}{{{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}$\hat{\theta}$}} in the $\tau$ coordinates

$\hat{\theta}$

angle corresponding to the boundary of the negativity region of the perturbation in the segment case, Equation (\ref{Equa:hat_theta_det_666})

$\hat{\zeta}$

convex curve for computing the formulas for the tent or arc case

$\hat{g}(s)$

Laplace transform of the kernel $g$

$\hat{L}$

length of the spiral between $Q_{0},\hat{Q}_{2}$ in the tent or arc computations

$\hat{P}_{0}$

initial point of the tent or arc perturbation

$\hat{Q}_{0}$

base point for $\hat{r}_{0}$ in the tent or arc perturbation

$\hat{Q}_{2}$

base point for $\hat{r}_{2}$ in the tent or arc perturbation

$\hat{r}_{0}$

entry ray for the formulas for tent or arc perturbation

$\hat{r}_{2}$

exit ray for the tent or arc computations

$\hat{R}$

curvature of the convex curve $\hat{\zeta}$ for the computations of the tent or arc formulas

$\imath$

imaginary unit

$\Im(z)$

imaginary part of the complex number $z$

$\kappa$

curvature of a curve in the plane, $\kappa=\frac{1}{R}$ being $R$ the radius of curvature

$\lambda$

eigenvalues of the RDE

$\mathbb{S}^{1}$

unit cicrle in $\mathbb{R}^{2}$

$\mathbf{t}^{-}(s),\mathbf{t}^{+}(s)$

left, rigth tangent to $\zeta(s)$

$\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{w}$

scalar product in $\mathbb{R}^{2}$

$\mathbf{v}\otimes\mathbf{v^{\prime}}$

tensor product of two vectors

$\mathbf{v}\wedge\mathbf{w}$

vector product in $\mathbb{R}^{2}$

$\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}$

generic vectors in $\mathbb{R}^{2}$

$\mathbf{v}^{\perp}$

vector rotated by $\frac{\pi}{2}$ counterclockwise

$\mathcal{A}(x)$

admissibility functional of the point $x$

$\mathcal{G}(\phi)$

primitive of the kernel G

$\mathcal{R}({\bar{\phi}})$

curve of minimal reachable points

$\mathcal{S}$

set of saturated points

$\mathcal{A}_{S}(Z,{\phi_{0}})$

set of admissible continutation of a spiral $Z$ from the angle ${\phi_{0}}$

$\mathcal{A}_{S}$

set of admissible spirals, Definition \ref{def:adm:spirals}

$\mathfrak{G}(\tau)$

primitive of $\mathfrak{g}$

$\mathfrak{g}(\tau)$

primitive of the function $g(\tau)e^{\bar{c}(2\pi+\bar{\alpha})(\tau+1)}$

$\partial^{-}\zeta(s)$

subdifferential of the curve $\zeta$

$\phi_{0}=(s^{+})^{-1}(s_{0})$

starting angle for the admissible family of spiral $\tilde{\zeta}(s,;s_{0})$

$\phi_{0}$

initial angle for the construction of the fastest/optimal closing spirals

$\mathbb{R}$

real numbers

$\Re(z)$

real part of the complex number $z$

$\sigma$

speed of construction of the barrier

$\theta$

variation of the direction of the spiral at $s_{0}$

$\tilde{\zeta}(s;s_{0})$

family of admissible spirals, parametrized by $s_{0}$

$\tilde{r}(\phi;s_{0})$

family of admissible spirals in the rotation angle coordinate $\phi$, parametrized by $s_{0}$

$\tilde{Z}_{\mathtt{a}}$

fastest saturated spiral, with the initial burning region $B_{\mathtt{a}}(0)$

$[P,Q]$

close segment with endpoints $P,Q$

$b(t)$

istantaneous burning rate

$G(\phi)$

Green kernel for the equation \eqref{eq:ODE:saturated}

$g(\tau)$

kernel for the linear Delay Differential Equation \eqref{Equa:rescal_RDE}

$g^{\mathrm{asympt}}(\tau)$

asymptotic expansion as $\tau\to\infty$ of the kernel $g$

$H(\phi)$

Heaviside function

$h_{1}(\Delta\phi)$

boundary of the first negativity region in the arc case

$K_{\mathrm{asympt}}$

constant for the linear part of the asymptotic behavior of the fastest saturated spiral

$L(\gamma)$

length of the curve $\gamma$

$M(\phi,\phi_{1},\phi_{2})$

kernel for the diffuse source \eqref{Equa:diffuse_source}

$m(\tau,\tau_{1},\tau_{2})$

kernel for the diffuse source of Lemma \ref{Lem:expli_source_RDE}

$N_{\mathrm{arc}}$

negative region for the perturbation of the arc case

$N_{\mathrm{res}}$

residual negative region for the arc case

$N_{\mathrm{segm}}$

negative region for the perturbation of the segment case

$r(\phi)$

length of the last segment of an optimal ray, Theorem \ref{Cor:angle_preprest}

$R(s)=R(\zeta(s))=R(\zeta;s)$

curvature of the line $\zeta$ in the point $\zeta(s)$

$R^{Z}(t)$

burned region at time $t$ by the fire constrained by the barrier $Z$

$r_{\mathrm{opt}}(\phi)$

the optimal solution for blocingk the fire at a given angle

$r_{\mathrm{sat}}(\phi)$

saturated spiral barrier

$s^{+}(\phi)$

final point of the last segment of an optimal ray, Theorem \ref{Cor:angle_preprest}

$s^{-}(\phi)$

initial point of the last segment of an optimal ray, Theorem \ref{Cor:angle_preprest}

$S_{\ell+1}$

star shaped region for the round decomposition

$u_{\xi}$

time function for the level set $u^{-1}(\zeta({\phi_{0}}))$

$u$

minimum time function

$V_{0}(\tau)$

inverse Laplace transform with poles in $s_{0}^{\pm}$

$x_{\mathrm{sl}}$

point on the curve $\mathtt{R}$ tangent to the saturated spiral

$y_{k}$

solutions to the eigenvalue equation \eqref{Equa:eigenvl_ima_1}

$Z$

admissible strategy (i.e. a rectifiable subset of the plane $\mathbb{R}^{2}$ with finite length)
